CM congruence and trivial zeros of the Katz p p -adic L L -functions for CM fields Thanks: Keywords Katz p p -adic L L -functions, congruences between p p -adic modular forms and deformations of Galois representations. Thanks: Betina was supported by the START-Prize Y966 of the Austrian Science Fund (FWF) and Hsieh was partially supported by MOST grants 108-2628-M-001-009-MY4 and 110-2628-M-001-004-.
Adel Betina and Ming-Lun Hsieh

Abstract
. The aim of this paper is to investigate the trivial zeros of the Katz p p -adic L L -functions by the CM congruence. We prove the existence of trivial zeros of the Katz p p -adic L L -functions for general CM fields and establish a first derivative formula of the cyclotomic p p -adic L L -functions at trivial zeros under some Leopoldt hypothesis. The crucial ingredients in our proof are a special case of p p -adic Kronecker limit formula for CM fields and a leading term formula of anticyclotomic p p -adic L L -functions at trivial zeros via the explicit congruences between CM and non-CM Hida families of Hilbert cusp forms.



2000 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F33, Secondary 11R23

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Conjectures on trivial zeros of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
F
be a totally real number field of degree
d
and absolute discriminant
\Delta_{F}
. Let
K
be a totally imaginary quadratic extension of
F
. Let
p
be a prime number. Fix once and for all embeddings
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}

singling out a decomposition subgroup of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}:=\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}/\mathbf{Q})
at
p
. Let
c\in\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K/F)
be the non-trivial automorphism. We suppose throughout this paper the following ordinary hypothesis on the CM field
K
:

(ord)

Every prime factor of
p
in
F
splits in
K
.

Let
\Sigma\subset\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(K,\mathbf{C})
be a
p
-ordinary CM-type of
K
, i.e.

•

\Sigma\sqcup\Sigma c=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(K,\mathbf{C})
and
\Sigma\cap\Sigma c=\emptyset
;

•

the set
\Sigma_{p}
of
p
-adic places induced by elements in
\Sigma
composed with
\iota_{p}\iota_{\infty}^{-1}
is disjoint from
\Sigma_{p}c
.

The hypothesis (
ord
) assures the existence of
p
-ordinary CM-types and we fix one such
\Sigma
. Denote by
\widetilde{K}_{\infty}
for the compositum of all the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extensions of
K
and by
K_{\Sigma_{p}}
for the maximal sub-extension of
\widetilde{K}_{\infty}
unramified outside
\Sigma_{p}
. In fact,
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{1+\delta_{F,p}}
and
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{d+1+\delta_{F,p}}
, where
\delta_{F,p}
is the Leopoldt defect for
F
and
p
. Let
{\mathcal{W}}
be a finite extension of the completion
\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\rm ur}
of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
in
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. To each choice of
p
-ordinary CM type
\Sigma
and a ray class character
\chi
of
K
valued in
{\mathcal{W}}
, one can attach the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\in{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)}\rrbracket
constructed by Katz
[
Kat78
]
and Hida–Tilouine
[
HT93
]
. This
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
is uniquely characterized by the following interpolation property: there exists a complex period

\Omega=(\Omega_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\in(\mathbf{C}^{\times})^{\Sigma}

and a
p
-adic period

\Omega_{p}=(\Omega_{p,\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\in({\mathcal{W}}^{\times})^{\Sigma}

such that for every crystalline character
\phi:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
of weight
k\Sigma+j(1-c)
for some integer
k
and
j=\sum\limits_{\sigma\in\Sigma}j_{\sigma}\sigma\in\mathbf{Z}[\Sigma]
with either
k>0
and
j_{\sigma}\geq 0
or
k\leq 1
and
j_{\sigma}+k\geq 1
, we have

(1.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{\phi}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi))}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma+2j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}^{\times}_{F}]}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}\cdot\frac{1}{(\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma+j}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(k\Sigma+j)L_{\rm fin}(0,\chi\phi_{\infty})}{\Omega^{k\Sigma+2j}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi\phi_{\infty}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}))(1-\chi\phi_{\infty}(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1}),

where

•

\nu_{\phi}:{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)}\rrbracket\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
is the unique
{\mathcal{W}}
-algebras homomorphism such that
\nu_{\phi\mid\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)}=\phi
,

•

\phi_{\infty}
is the grossencharacter of type
A_{0}
associated with
\phi
such that
\phi_{\infty}(\mathfrak{Q})
is the value of
\phi
at the geometric Frobenius
{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{Q}}
at primes
\mathfrak{Q}\nmid p
,

•

L_{\rm fin}(s,\chi\phi_{\infty})=\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\chi\phi_{\infty}({\mathfrak{a}}){\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{-s}
is the classical
L
-series of
\chi\phi_{\infty}
, where
{\mathfrak{a}}
runs over all integral ideals of
K
relatively prime to the conductor of
\chi\phi_{\infty}
.

•

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}=c(\mathfrak{P})
.

In the expression of (
1.1
), we have used the convention for
\xi=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\xi_{\sigma}\sigma\in\mathbf{Z}[\Sigma]
,
and for
x=(x_{\sigma})\in\mathbf{C}^{\Sigma}
:

x^{\xi}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}x_{\sigma}^{\xi_{\sigma}};\quad\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(\xi)=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}2(2\pi)^{-\xi_{\sigma}}\Gamma(\xi_{\sigma}).

Any element
a\in\mathbf{C}
is considered to be an element of
\mathbf{C}^{\Sigma}
via the diagonal embedding
a\mapsto(a)_{\sigma\in\Sigma}
.

For each positive integer
m
, denote by
\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{m})
the
m
-th cyclotomic field. Let
\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})=\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})
and denote by
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbf{Q})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the usual
p
-adic cyclotomic character. Let
\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}\subset\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})
be the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
\mathbf{Q}
. We view
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbf{Q})
as a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbf{Q})
via the isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbf{Q})\simeq\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbf{Q})\times\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p})/\mathbf{Q}),\,\sigma\mapsto(\sigma|_{\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}},\sigma|_{\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p})})
. Let
K_{\infty}^{+}=\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}K
be the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
and let
\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the character defined by the composition

\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\widetilde{K}_{\infty}/K)\to\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\infty}^{+}/K)\to\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbf{Q})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbf{Q})\overset{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}.

We consider the cyclotomic Katz
p
-adic
L
-function defined by

L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi):=\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>^{s}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\right),\quad s\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}.

Let us put

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\mid\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})=1\right\};

\displaystyle r_{\Sigma}(\chi)

\displaystyle=\#\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}-\dim_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\!\!{\rm H}^{0}(K,\chi).

In view of the interpolation formula (
1.1
), we expect that
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
has a
trivial zero
whenever
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>0
. By analogy to a conjecture of Gross regarding the order of vanishing of Deligne-Ribet
p
-adic
L
-functions, we have the following conjecture regarding the order of the vanishing of
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
at
s=0
.

Conjecture 1
.

We always have

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)=r_{\Sigma}(\chi).

Note that when
K
is an imaginary quadratic field, the implication
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>0\Rightarrow L_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)=0
follows from the
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula, but in general it is not clear if
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
possesses a zero at
s=0
when
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>0
since
s=0
is a not a critical point for
\chi
and is outside the range of the interpolation of
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
.

We briefly recall the conjectural formula of the leading term of
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
at the trivial zero in
[
BS19
]
. First we prepare some crucial ingredients in the statement of this formula. Following the notation in
[
Gro81
]
, if
X
is an abelian group and
R
is a ring, we denote the
R
-module
R\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}X
simply by
RX
. Let
H:=\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\chi}
be the finite cyclic extension of
K
cut out by
\chi
and
G:=\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(H/K)
. Let
\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}
the group of units of
H
. Put

\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]:=e_{\chi}\left(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}\right).

where
e_{\chi}
is the usual idempotent

e_{\chi}=\frac{1}{[H:K]}\sum_{\tau\in G}\chi(\tau^{-1})\otimes\tau.

Let
\log_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
denote the Iwasawa’s
p
-adic logarithm with
\log_{p}(p)=0
. Let
\Sigma_{H}
be the set of embeddings
\sigma_{H}:H\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}
extending those in
\Sigma
and let
G
act on
\Sigma_{H}
by
\tau\cdot\sigma=\sigma\circ\tau^{-1}
for
\tau\in G
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{H}
. Let
Y_{H,\Sigma}
be the free abelian group on
\Sigma_{H}
, regarded as a
\mathbf{Z}[G]
-module with the the action of
G
-action on
\Sigma_{H}
. Define the
p
-adic regulator map

\log_{\Sigma,p}:H^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}Y_{H,\Sigma},\quad\log_{\Sigma,p}(x)=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{H}}\log_{p}(\iota_{p}\sigma(x))\sigma.

We extend the map
\log_{\Sigma,p}
by linearity to the map
\log_{\Sigma,p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}H^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}Y_{H,\Sigma}
of
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[G]
-modules and obtain a map between the
\chi
-isotypic parts on the both sides

(1.2)

\log_{\Sigma,p}:\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]\to Y_{H,\Sigma}[\chi]:=e_{\chi}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}Y_{H,\Sigma}).

Conjecture 2

(The
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
)
.

The map
\log_{\Sigma,p}
in (
1.2
) is injective.

We remark that Conjecture
2
is a consequence of the well-known
p
-adic Schanuel conjecture as explained in
[
HT94
, Lemma 1.2.1]
; the four exponential conjecture implies Conjecture
2
when
F
is a real quadratic field. Fixing an isomorphism
\iota:\mathbf{C}\simeq\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
such that
\iota\circ\iota_{\infty}=\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, the Dirichlet regulator map is defined by

\log_{\Sigma,\infty}:H^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}Y_{H,\Sigma},\quad\log_{\Sigma,\infty}(x)=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{H}}\iota\left(\log\left|\sigma(x)\right|\right)\sigma.

Suppose that
\chi
is not the trivial character
{\mathbf{1}}
. By Dirichlet’s units Theorem, the induced map
\log_{\Sigma,\infty}:\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Y_{H,\Sigma}[\chi]
is an isomorphism of
d
-dimensional
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-vector spaces. It follows that if Conjecture
2
holds, then the
p
-adic regulator map
\log_{\Sigma,p}:\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]\simeq Y_{H,\Sigma}[\chi]
is also an isomorphism. Define the regulator
\mathscr{R}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
in
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
by

\mathscr{R}_{\Sigma}(\chi):=\det\left(\log_{\Sigma,p}\circ\log_{\Sigma,\infty}^{-1}|\,Y_{H,\Sigma}[\chi]\right).

It is clear that the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
is equivalent to the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{R}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
. Following Buyukboduk and Sakamoto
[
BS19
, Definition 5.2]
, we next introduce the cyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant for
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
under the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture. Let
\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}
be the
\overline{\Sigma}_{p}
-units in
H
and put

\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}[\chi]:=e_{\chi}\left(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}\right).

We consider the space

\mathbf{U}_{\chi}:=\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\left\{\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}[\chi]\overset{\log_{\Sigma,p}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}Y_{H,\Sigma}\right\}.

If
\mathfrak{P}
is a prime ideal of the ring
\mathfrak{o}_{K}
of integers of
K
, let
{\mathrm{ord}}_{\mathfrak{P}}:K_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\times}\to\mathbf{Z}
be the normalized valuation map and
\log_{\mathfrak{P}}:=\log_{p}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{K_{\mathfrak{P}}/\mathbf{Q}_{p}}:K_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\times}\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}
be the cyclotomic
p
-adic logarithm, where
{\mathrm{N}}_{K_{\mathfrak{P}}/\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
is the norm map from
K_{\mathfrak{P}}
to
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. For each
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
, we choose a prime
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{H}
of
H
above
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
. Then via the inclusion
\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}\subset H^{\times}\subset H_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{H}}^{\times}=K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\times}
, we can evaluate
{\mathrm{ord}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}
and
\log_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}
on
\mathfrak{o}_{H,\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}^{\times}
and extend by linearity to maps

\displaystyle{\mathbf{O}}_{p}:\mathbf{U}_{\chi}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}{\mathrm{ord}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}};

\displaystyle{\mathbf{L}}_{p}:\mathbf{U}_{\chi}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}\log_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}.

If
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
and the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
holds,
the first map
{\bf O}_{p}
is an isomorphism; we can define the cyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant of
\chi
by

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}:=(-1)^{r_{\Sigma}(\chi)}\det\left({\bf O}_{p}^{-1}\circ\mathbf{L}_{p}\right).

Note that the definition of
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}
does not depend on the choice of the prime
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{H}
above
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
.

For any
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\Gamma}
of
K
with the Galois group
\Gamma
, we can also consider the trivial zeros of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)|_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket
obtained by the restriction of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
to
\Gamma
.
It is shown in
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.1]
that the leading term formula of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)|_{\Gamma}
at the trivial zero can be obtained as a consequence of several outstanding open conjectures in algebraic number theory, eg. Rubin-Stark conjecture and the Reciprocity Conjecture. The following is the special case of this conjectural leading term formula when
K_{\Gamma}=K_{\infty}^{+}
is the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension.

Conjecture 3
.

Assume the validity of the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
and
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
. Then we have
{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)\geq r_{\Sigma}(\chi)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to 0}\frac{L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)}{s^{r_{\Sigma}(\chi)}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r_{\Sigma}(\chi)}\mathscr{L}_{\chi}\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\Sigma_{p}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}))

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1})\cdot\mathscr{R}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\cdot L_{\rm fin}^{*}(0,\chi),

where
L_{\rm fin}^{*}(0,\chi):=\iota\left(\lim\limits_{s\to 0}s^{-d}L_{\rm fin}(s,\chi)\right)\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
is the leading term of the
L
-series
L_{\rm fin}(s,\chi)
.

When
K
is an imaginary quadratic field (so
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)\leq 1
), Conjecture
1
is equivalent to the non-vanishing of the
\mathscr{L}
-invariant, which is known to be a consequence of the four exponentials conjecture (
[
BD21
, Prop. 1.11]
), Conjecture
2
holds thanks to the Baker-Brumer Theorem and Conjecture
3
is proved in
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.6]
via the Euler system of elliptic units. These conjectures are still wide open for general CM fields.

1.2.
Statement of the main results

The main goal of this article is to prove the existence of the trivial zeros and provide some evidence towards Conjecture
1
for general CM fields in the rank zero and one case under the Leopoldt hypotheses. Our method does not reply on the existence of conjectural Rubin-Stark units and conjectural reciprocity law. In the course of the proofs, we also obtain a leading term formula of the
anticyclotomic
Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Let
h_{K/F}
be the relative class number of
K/F
and
\mathfrak{D}_{K/F}
be the relative different of
K/F
. For simplicity, it is assumed throughout this introduction that
(\chi,K,p)
satisfies the following technical conditions.

(H1)

\displaystyle\text{ The prime }p\text{ does not divide }6\Delta_{F}h_{K/F};

(H2)

\displaystyle\text{$\chi$ is of prime-to-$p$ order and is unramified at primes dividing $p\mathfrak{D}_{K/F}$}.

Note that these assumptions are satisfied by all but finitely primes
p
once
(\chi,K)
is fixed. Our first result gives a sufficient and necessary condition for the existence of the trivial zero, i.e. Conjecture
1
in the rank zero case under the following Leopoldt hypothesis.

Hypothesis (L)
.

The Leopoldt conjecture for
F
and the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
both hold.

Theorem A
.

Suppose that Hypothesis (L) is valid. Then we have

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)=0\text{ if and only if }r_{\Sigma}(\chi)=0.

We say
\chi
is
anticyclotomic
if
\chi(c\sigma c)=\chi(\sigma^{-1})
for
\sigma\in\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\overline{K}/K)
. If
\chi
is further assumed to be anticyclotomic, it was shown in
[
BDF21
]
that
L_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)\neq 0
if
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)=0
under Hypothesis (L). Our second result relates the rank one case of Conjecture
1
to the non-vanishing of the
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}
for anticyclotomic characters
\chi
.

Theorem B
.

In addition to the validity of Hypothesis (L), we assume further that
\chi
is a non-trivial anticyclotomic character. Then we have

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)=1\text{ if and only if }r_{\Sigma}(\chi)=1\text{ and }\mathscr{L}_{\chi}\neq 0.

1.3.
The method

Although our results are mainly concerned with the first derivative of the cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function, the method crucially relies on the use of certain two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function, which we describe as follows. Denote by
\mathscr{V}:G_{F}^{\rm ab}\to G_{K}^{\rm ab}
for the transfer map and fix a
p
-adic character
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}\circ\mathscr{V}
induces the
p
-adic cyclotomic character of
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)
. Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\overline{\Sigma}}
denote the conjugate character
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{c}
and define the analytic function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(-,-,\chi):\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
by

(1.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,t,\chi):

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\overline{\Sigma}}^{t}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\right),\quad(s,t)\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}.

We prove the following result in Theorem
4.9
and Proposition
6.6
.

Theorem 1.1
.

There exists an analytic function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
on
\mathbf{Z}_{p}-\left\{0\right\}
such that

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)={\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}})).

Moreover, the following statements hold:

(i)

If the Leopoldt conjecture for
F
holds, then
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
is analytic at
s=0
if
\chi
is non-trivial, and has a simple pole at
s=0
if
\chi={\mathbf{1}}
is the trivial character.

(ii)

If Hypothesis (L) is valid, then
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)\neq 0
for
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
.

By definition, we have
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,s,\chi)=L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
for
s
in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
0
, and hence Theorem
A
directly follows from Theorem
1.1
. We shall call
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
the
improved

p
-adic
L
-function for
\chi
in the sequel. The proof of Theorem
1.1
is briefly outlined as follows. Recall that the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
for CM fields is obtained by the evaluation of Katz’s Eisenstein measure at CM points. To construct this improved
p
-adic
L
-function, we modify the Katz’s
p
-adic Eisenstein measure and construct a nice
ordinary

p
-adic Eisenstein measure; then obtain the improved
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
by evaluating this
p
-adic ordinary Eisenstein measure at CM points. The interpolation formula of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
is proved via an explicit calculation of the toric period integral of Eisenstein series (Proposition
4.1
), and then the relation between
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
can be seen immediately from the interpolation formula. The analyticity of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
is determined by that of the constant term of our ordinary
p
-adic Eisenstein series, which is essentially the Deligne-Ribet
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}(1-s,\chi_{+}^{-1})
with
\chi_{+}:=\chi\circ\mathscr{V}
. We thus need the Leopoldt conjecture for
F
to conclude the analyticity of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
at
s=0
in some special cases (See Proposition
4.8
). To see the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)
, we first observe that the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture is equivalent to the vanishing of the Bloch-Kato Selmer group
{\rm H}^{1}_{(\emptyset,f)}(K,\chi)
, consisting classes in
{\rm H}^{1}(K,\chi)
unramified outside
\Sigma_{p}
(Lemma
2.1
). On the other hand, we deduce a one-sided divisibility in the main conjecture for the improved
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
from
[
Hsi14a
]
, which in turn implies that
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)\neq 0
if
{\rm H}^{1}_{(\emptyset,f)}(K,\chi)=\left\{0\right\}
. This allows us to conclude the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)
under the Hypothesis (L).

In general the value
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)
is expected to be always nonzero whenever
\chi
is non-trivial. When
K
is an imaginary quadratic field,
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)
is nothing but the
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{*}(s,\chi)
introduced in
[
CH22
, §3.3]
, and the value
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)
equals the
p
-adic logarithm of Robert’s units by the
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula (
cf.

[
dS87
, Theorem 5.2, page 88]
,
[
CH22
, (3.5)]
). In this case, the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)
follows from the Baker-Brumer Theorem. Since the generalization of Katz’
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula to CM fields is not available yet, the value
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)
remains very mysterious. Nonetheless, Conjecture
3
leads us to propose the following

Conjecture 4
.

If
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
, then we have

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)=\mathscr{R}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1})\cdot L_{\rm fin}^{*}(0,\chi).

Now we turn to the rank one case of Conjecture
1
for anticyclotomic characters. We prove the following first derivative formula of
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
in Corollary
6.5
. Combined with the non-vanishing of
{\mathcal{L}}^{*}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)
in Theorem
1.1
, this formula yields our Theorem
B
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
\chi
be a non-trivial anticyclotomic character. Suppose that the Hypothesis (L) holds. If
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>0
and
\mathfrak{P}_{1}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
, then
L_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)=0
, and

\frac{L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)}{s}\Big|_{s=0}=\mathscr{L}_{\chi}\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\left\{\mathfrak{P}_{1}\right\}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})).

When
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>1
, Theorem
1.2
amounts to the statement
L^{\prime}_{\Sigma}(0,\chi)=0
. Our proof of Theorem
1.2
is a vast generalization of the method in
[
CH22
]
. One of the key ingredients is to establish a leading term formula of the anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)
, whose proof relies on (i) a refinement of the method of Hida and Tillouine
[
HT93
]
to produce non-trivial CM congruence by the
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg convolution, and (ii) the clever argument in
[
DKV18
]
of relating the leading term of the
U_{p}
-eigenvalues of
\Lambda
-adic generalized Hecke eigenforms to the
\mathscr{L}
-invariants. Let us sketch the idea of the proof. First note that since
\chi
is anticyclotomic, we can choose a ray class character
\phi
such that
\phi^{1-c}=\chi
. For a well-chosen such character
\phi
, we generalize the explicit CM congruence among elliptic modular forms in
[
CH22
]
to the Hilbert case and construct an explicit ordinary
{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{X}\rrbracket
-adic Hilbert cusp forms
\mathscr{H}
in the sense of
[
Wil88
, §1.2]
such that

•

\mathscr{H}
is a generalized Hecke eigenform modulo
X^{r+1}
with
r=r_{\Sigma}(\chi)
;

•

\mathscr{H}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r}})
is congruent to the Hida family
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}
of CM forms associated with
\phi^{c}
.

After an involved calculation in the Rankin-Selberg convolution (Proposition
5.3
), we further find an explicit relation between
U_{p}
-eigenvalues of
\mathscr{H}
and the following product of
p
-adic
L
-functions

\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi)}\cdot\frac{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,{\mathbf{1}})},

where
\zeta_{F,p}(s)
is the
p
-adic Dekekind zeta function for
F
. We prove a special case of the
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula

\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,{\mathbf{1}})}{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)}\Big|_{s=0}=2^{-d}\cdot h_{K/F}

in Proposition
4.8
, and then apply the method of
[
DKV18
]
to show in Theorem
6.4
that

{\mathrm{ord}}_{s=0}{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)\geq r_{\Sigma}(\chi)

and prove the following leading term formula of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)
at
s=0

\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)}{s^{r_{\Sigma}(\chi)}}\Big|_{s=0}=\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(0,\chi)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\Sigma_{p}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})),

where
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}
is the anticyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant (See Definition
2.2
). We thus deduce in Corollary
6.5
the first derivative formula of
L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)
from the equation

\frac{L_{\Sigma}(s,\chi)}{s}\Big|_{s=0}=2\cdot\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{s}\Big|_{s=0}-\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)}{s}\Big|_{s=0}

as well as the relation between the
{\mathcal{L}}
-invariants
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}
.

Remark 1.3
.

The role of the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture in the research on trivial zeros of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions is fundamental. In the Appendix, we use Roy’s strong six exponential theorem to show that if
F
is a real quadratic field where
p
is split and
\chi\neq\chi^{c}
, then there exists a
p
-ordinary CM-type
\Sigma
such that the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
holds. In general, to the best of the authors’ knowledge, this conjecture is only known when
H
is an abelian extension of an imaginary quadratic field where
p
splits.

This paper is organized as follows. In §
2
, we recall the general definition of
\mathscr{L}
-invariants along any
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
. In §
3
we review necessary background in the theory of Hilbert modular forms and Hilbert modular varieties. In §
4
, we construct a family of special Eisenstein series
{\bf E}_{\lambda}
indexed by certain Hecke characters
\lambda
of
K
via the adelic approach described in
[
Jac72
, §19]
and prove a Damerell’s formula in Theorem
4.2
. The desired
p
-adic Eisenstein measure is constructed in Proposition
4.7
. In §
5
, we apply the
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg method to construct the
\Lambda
-adic form
\mathscr{H}
in Definition
5.2
and state in Proposition
5.3
the explicit formula of the Rankin-Selberg convolution. In §
6
, we make use of the elegant ideas in
[
DKV18
]
to give a relation between the
U_{p}
-eigenvalues of generalized Hecke eigenforms and the
\mathscr{L}
-invariant in Proposition
6.1
and prove the leading term formula of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)
. Finally in §
7
we complete the proof of Proposition
5.3
. In the Appendix, we explain the connection between
p
-adic transcendental conjectures and the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture.

Acknowledgements.
The first named author (A.B.) would like to thank D. Benois, M. Dimitrov, A. Maksoud and S.C Shih for numerous stimulating discussions. The second author (M.H.) thanks K. Buyukboduk for helpful comments on an early version of the manuscript.

Notation and conventions

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{p}(1)
denote the
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
representation of dimension
1
on which
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
acts by the
p
-adic cyclotomic character. We follow the geometric convention: the Hodge-Tate weight of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}(1)
is
-1
.

If
L
is a local or global field of characteristic zero, let
\mathfrak{o}_{L}
be the ring of integers of
L
. Let
G_{L}
denote the absolute Galois group of
L
and let
C_{L}:=L^{\times}
if
L
is local and
C_{L}
be the idele class group
\mathbf{A}_{L}^{\times}/L^{\times}
if
L
is global. Let
\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{L}:C_{L}\to G_{L}^{ab}
be the
geometrically normalized
reciprocity law homomorphism.

Let
L
be a number field. If
\mathfrak{q}
is a prime ideal of
\mathfrak{o}_{L}
(resp.
v
is a place of
L
),
let
L_{\mathfrak{q}}
(resp.
L_{v}
) be the completion of
L
at
\mathfrak{q}
(resp.
v
).
Then
\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{L_{\mathfrak{q}}}:L_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\times}\to G_{L_{\mathfrak{q}}}^{ab}
sends a uniformizer
\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}
of
\mathfrak{o}_{L_{\mathfrak{q}}}
to the corresponding geometric Frobenius
{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. If
S
is a finite set of prime ideals of
\mathfrak{o}_{L}
, let
L(S)
be the maximal algebraic extension of
L
unramified outside
S
and let
G_{L,S}=\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(L(S)/L)
. For a fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of a global field
L
, we let
{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\prod_{\mathfrak{q}}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{n_{\mathfrak{q}}}
if
{\mathfrak{a}}
has the prime ideal factorization
\prod_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{q}^{n_{\mathfrak{q}}}
.

For any finite extension of global (or local) fields
E/L
, we let
{\mathrm{N}}_{E/L}:E\to L
be the norm map and let
\mathfrak{d}_{E}\subset\mathfrak{o}_{E}
denotes the absolute different ideal of
E
.

2.
The
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

2.1.
The
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture

Let
S_{p}(K)
be the set of primes of
K
lying above
p
. Recall that
\Sigma\subset\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(K,\mathbf{C})
is a fixed CM type and
\Sigma_{p}\subset S_{p}(K)
is the subset of primes induced by
\iota_{p}\iota_{\infty}^{-1}\circ\Sigma
(one has
S_{p}(K)=\Sigma_{p}\coprod\overline{\Sigma}_{p}
).

If
A
is a continuous
G_{K}
-module, then for any subgroup
{\mathcal{L}}\subset{\rm H}^{1}(K_{p},A)=\oplus_{w\mid p}{\rm H}^{1}(K_{w},A)
, we let
{\rm H}^{1}(K,A)
be the Selmer group of
A
with the local condition
{\mathcal{L}}
defined by

{\rm H}^{1}_{\mathcal{L}}(K,A)=\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\left\{{\rm H}^{1}(K,A)\to\prod_{w\nmid p}{\rm H}^{1}(I_{w},A)\times\frac{{\rm H}^{1}(K_{p},A)}{{\mathcal{L}}}\right\}.

For
\mathfrak{P}\in S_{p}(K)
, let
{\rm H}^{1}_{\emptyset}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)={\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)
and
{\rm H}^{1}_{0}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)=\left\{0\right\}
. Denote by
{\rm H}^{1}_{f}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)
be the finite part of
{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)
in the sense of Bloch and Kato. Put

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{a,b}=

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}{\rm H}^{1}_{a}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},A)\oplus{\rm H}^{1}_{b}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}},A)\text{ for }a,b\in\left\{0,f,\emptyset\right\}.

We set

{\rm H}^{1}_{(a,b)}(K,A):={\rm H}^{1}_{{\mathcal{L}}_{a,b}}(K,A).

Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and
\chi:{\rm G}_{K}\to E^{\times}
be a non-trivial ray class character of
K
. Set
H
be the finite extension of
K
cut out by
\chi
and
C=\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(H/K)
. The Kummer map gives rise to an isomorphism

(2.1)

\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1)),

where
\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]
is the
\chi
-isotypic component

\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]:=\{x\in E\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}\mid 1\otimes g(x)=\chi(g)\otimes 1\cdot x\text{ for all }g\in\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(H/K)\}.

Let

(2.2)

\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}=\left\{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\mid\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})=1\right\}

be the set of
irregular
primes for
\chi
in
\Sigma_{p}
. Let
d:=[F:\mathbf{Q}]
and
r_{\Sigma}(\chi)=\#\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
. By Dirichlet’s units Theorem and a standard calculation of local Galois cohomology groups, it is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))

\displaystyle=d+r_{\Sigma}(\chi),

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in\Sigma_{p}}\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{f}(K_{w},\chi^{-1}(1))=d,

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))

\displaystyle\leq r_{\Sigma}(\chi)+\dim{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1)).

Note that by definition, the subspace
{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))
is actually the kernel of the
p
-adic regulator map

\log_{\Sigma,p}:{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))\simeq\mathfrak{o}_{H}^{\times}[\chi]\to EY_{H,\Sigma},\quad x\mapsto\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{H}}\log_{p}(\iota_{p}\sigma(x))\sigma;

therefore the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture (Conjecture
2
) can be rephrased as

{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))=\left\{0\right\}.

Lemma 2.1
.

The
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
is equivalent to

(
\Sigma
-Leo)

{\rm H}^{1}_{(\emptyset,f)}(K,\chi)=\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\left\{{\rm H}^{1}(K,\chi)\to\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{Q}\in\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}{\rm H}^{1}(I_{\mathfrak{Q}},\chi)\right\}=\left\{0\right\}.

Proof.
.

Since
{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi)=\left\{0\right\}
by the finiteness of the class numbers, by the Poitou-Tate exact sequence we have

0\to{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))\to{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))\to\bigoplus_{w\in\Sigma_{p}}{\rm H}^{1}_{f}(K_{w},\chi^{-1}(1))\to{\rm H}^{1}_{(\emptyset,f)}(K,\chi)^{\vee}\to 0,

where
V^{\vee}=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{E}(V,E)
for a
E
-vector space
V
. We thus find that
\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(\emptyset,f)}(K,\chi)=\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))
; the assertion follows.
∎

2.2.
The
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

We recall the general definition of
\mathscr{L}
-invariants for CM fields following
[
BS19
, Definition 5.2]
. The validity of the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt conjecture for
\chi
is assumed in this subsection. So we have
\dim_{E}{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))=r_{\Sigma}(\chi)
and the decomposition

{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))={\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))\bigoplus{\rm H}^{1}_{(f,f)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1)).

We shall define the
\mathscr{L}
-invariant for
\chi
to be certain
p
-adic regulator of the cohomology group
{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))
. For every
\mathfrak{P}\in S_{p}(K)
, let
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathfrak{P}}:{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},\chi^{-1}(1))\times{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{P}},\chi)\to E
be the local Tate pairing. For
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
,
\chi|_{G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}}=1
, and we denote by
{\mathrm{ord}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}\in{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}},\chi)=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}^{\times},E)
the usual
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
-adic valuation. Let
X_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}
be the free abelian group generated by
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
with
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
and write
EX_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}=E\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}X_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{O}_{p}:{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))

\displaystyle\to EX_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}},\quad\mathbf{O}_{p}(x)=\sum_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}\langle{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}(x),{\mathrm{ord}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}\rangle_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}.

Note that
{\rm H}^{1}_{f}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{Q}}},\chi^{-1}(1))={\rm H}^{1}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{Q}}},\chi^{-1}(1))
if
\mathfrak{Q}\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
, so the
\Sigma
-Leopodlt conjecture implies that
\mathbf{O}_{p}
is injective and hence an isomorphism. On the other hand, for each
\psi\in{\rm H}^{1}(K,E)=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(G_{K},E)
, we put

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{p}^{\psi}:{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))

\displaystyle\to EX_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}},\quad\mathbf{L}_{p}^{\psi}(x)=\sum_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}\langle{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}(\psi),{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}(x)\rangle_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}.

Definition 2.2
.

The
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}
associated with the ray class character
\chi
along the additive homomorphism
\psi:G_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
is defined by

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}:=(-1)^{r_{\Sigma}(\chi)}\det(\mathbf{L}_{p}^{\psi}\circ\mathbf{O}_{p}^{-1}|EX_{\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}).

By definition, the cyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}:=\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\ell^{\rm cyc}}
with
\ell^{\rm cyc}=\log_{p}\circ\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>
. We define the
anticyclotomic

\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}
by

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}:=\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\ell^{\rm ac}},\quad\ell^{\rm ac}:=\log_{p}\circ\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{1-c}:G_{K}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}.

3.
Review of Hilbert modular varieties and Hilbert modular forms

We will use the following notation frequently. If
M
is an finitely generated abelian group, denote by
\widehat{M}
the profinite completion of
M
. If
E
is a number field, denote by
\mathfrak{o}_{E}
the ring of integers of
E
. Let
\widehat{E}=\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Q}
be the group of finite ideles. If
a\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
, the fractional ideal of
E
generated by
a
is defined to be
a\mathfrak{o}_{E}:=a\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\cap E
, and if
{\mathfrak{a}}
is an integral ideal of
E
, let
{\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{a}}:=\#(\mathcal{O}_{E}/{\mathfrak{a}})
be the norm of
{\mathfrak{a}}
.

3.1.
Hilbert Shimura varieties

Let
F
be a totally real field and let
\mathfrak{o}=\mathfrak{o}_{F}
. Recall that a Hilbert-Blumenthal abelian variety by
\mathfrak{o}
(or a HBAV for short) over a scheme
S
is an abelian scheme
A
over
S
of relative dimension
d
and equipped with an embedding of rings
\iota:\mathfrak{o}\to\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits_{S}(A)
such that
\mathop{\rm Lie}\nolimits(A)
is an
\mathfrak{o}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module locally free of rank
1
.

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}
be a fractional ideal of
F
and let
{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}
be the set of totally positive elements in
{\mathfrak{c}}
. The dual abelian scheme
A^{\rm t}{}_{/S}
of
A
has a canonical HBAV structure. Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{A}:=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{o}}(A,A^{\rm t})_{\rm sym}
be the rank
1
projective
\mathfrak{o}
-module of symmetric
\mathfrak{o}
-linear homorphisms and
{\mathcal{P}}_{A}^{+}\subset{\mathcal{P}}_{A}
be the positive cone of polarizations. A
{\mathfrak{c}}
-polarization of
A
is an
\mathfrak{o}
-linear map
\lambda:{\mathfrak{c}}\to{\mathcal{P}}_{A}
sending
{\mathfrak{c}}_{+}
to
{\mathcal{P}}_{A}^{+}
such that the morphism

\lambda:A\otimes_{\mathfrak{o}}{\mathfrak{c}}\simeq A^{\rm t},\quad x\otimes\alpha\mapsto\lambda(\alpha)x.

is an isomorphism of abelian schemes.

Let
N
be a positive integer and let
\zeta_{N}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{N}}
be a fixed primitive
N
-th root of unity. Suppose that
N\geq 4
is coprime to
p
and the fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{c}}
is coprime to
pN
. Consider the moduli functor
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n}}
over
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
classifying HBAV with
{\mathfrak{c}}
-polarizations and level
\Gamma(N)\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{n})
-structures. More precisely, for a basis scheme
S
over
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n}}(S)
is the set of isomorphism classes of the quadruple
(A,\lambda,\iota,\eta_{N},j_{p})_{/S}
, where

•

A
a HBAV over
S
and
\lambda
is a
{\mathfrak{c}}
-polarization of
A
.

•

\eta_{N}:(\mathfrak{o}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}N^{-1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z})\oplus(\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mu_{N})\simeq A[N]
is an
\mathfrak{o}
-linear isomorphism

•

j:\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mu_{p^{n}}{}_{/S}\hookrightarrow A[p^{n}]
is an
\mathfrak{o}
-linear closed immersion of group schemes over
S
.

The functor
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n}}
is represented by a quasi-projective and smooth scheme
\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n})
of relative dimension
[F:\mathbf{Q}]
over
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
(
[
Rap78
]
,
[
Cha90
]
and
[
DP94
]
). We put

\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{\infty})=\varprojlim_{n\to\infty}\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n}).

3.2.
Complex uniformizaton

Now we recall the complex analytic structure of
\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n})(\mathbf{C})
.
Let
{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\subset F\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{C}
be the upper half plane defined by

{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}=\left\{\tau=x+\sqrt{-1}y\in F\otimes\mathbf{C}\mid x\in F\otimes\mathbf{R},\,y\in(F\otimes\mathbf{R})_{+}\right\}.

To each
x=(\tau_{x},g_{x})\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})
, we associate the quadruple
(A_{x},\lambda_{x},\eta_{x}^{(p)},j_{x})
over
\mathbf{C}
as follows: Let
V=Fe_{1}\oplus Fe_{2}
be the two-dimensional vector space over
F
with the symplectic pairing

\langle ae_{1}+be_{2},ce_{1}+de_{2}\rangle=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits_{F/\mathbf{Q}}(bc-ad).

Let
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F)
act on
V
from the right via

(xe_{1}+ye_{2})\begin{pmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{pmatrix}=(xa+yc)e_{1}+(xb+yd)e_{2}.

For
\tau\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}
, define the
\mathfrak{o}
-linear isomorphism
{\rm q}_{\tau}:V\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F\otimes\mathbf{C}
by

{\rm q}_{\tau}(xe_{1}+ye_{2})=x\tau+y,\quad(x,y\in F\otimes\mathbf{R}).

Let
\mathbf{L}
be the lattice
\mathfrak{o}e_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}e_{2}
. For
g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})
, put

\mathbf{L}_{g}:=(\mathbf{L}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\widehat{\mathbf{Z}})g^{-1}\cap V.

Given
(\tau_{x},g_{x})\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})
, we define the complex torus

A_{x}(\mathbf{C})=(F\otimes\mathbf{C})/L_{x},\quad L_{x}={\rm q}_{\tau_{x}}(\mathbf{L}_{g_{x}}).

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}_{x}
be the fractional ideal
\det g_{x}\mathfrak{o}
generated by
\det g_{x}
.
For
a\in{\mathfrak{c}}_{x}
, the pairing
E_{a}(z,w):=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits_{F/\mathbf{Q}}(a\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(\overline{z}w)/\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\tau_{x})
defines a Riemann form on
F\otimes\mathbf{C}
if
a\in({\mathfrak{c}}_{x})_{+}
. Therefore
A_{x}(\mathbf{C})
carries a structure of HBAV
A_{x}
over
\mathbf{C}
with the
\mathfrak{o}
-action induced by
{\rm q}_{\tau_{x}}
. For
a\in{\mathfrak{c}}_{x}
, let
\lambda_{x}(a):A_{x}\to A_{x}^{\rm t}
be the isogeny induced by
E_{a}(-,-)
, and the map
\lambda_{x}:a\mapsto\lambda_{x}(a)
induces a
{\mathfrak{c}}_{x}
-polarization
\lambda:({\mathfrak{c}}_{x},({\mathfrak{c}}_{x})_{+})\to({\mathcal{P}}_{A_{x}},{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{x}}^{+})
. Define the level
\Gamma(N)\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{n})
-structures by

\displaystyle\eta_{x}

\displaystyle:(\mathfrak{o}\otimes N^{-1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z})\oplus(\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes\mu_{N})\simeq A_{x}[N],

\displaystyle\qquad(a\otimes N^{-1},b\otimes\zeta_{N})\mapsto(\frac{a}{N}e_{1}g^{-1}+\frac{b}{N}e_{2}g^{-1})\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathbf{L}_{x}}),

\displaystyle j_{x}

\displaystyle:\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes\mu_{p^{n}}\hookrightarrow A_{x}[p^{n}],\quad a\otimes\zeta_{p^{n}}\mapsto a/p^{n}e_{2}g_{p}^{-1}\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathbf{L}_{g}}).

This way
(A_{x},\lambda_{x},,\eta_{x},j_{x})
is a HBAV with
{\mathfrak{c}}_{x}
-polarization
\lambda_{x}
and
\Gamma(N)\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{n})
-structures, and the isomorphism class
[(A_{x},\lambda_{x},,\eta_{x},j_{x})]
gives rise to a point in
\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}}_{x},N,p^{n})(\mathbf{C})
.

Fix an idele
\mathbf{c}\in\widehat{F}^{(pN){\times}}
such that
\mathbf{c}\mathfrak{o}={\mathfrak{c}}
and put

\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})^{(\mathbf{c})}=\left\{g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})\mid\det g=\mathbf{c}\right\}.

For any open-compact subgroup in
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})
, consider the complex Hilbert modular variety
Sh(\mathbf{c},U)_{/\mathbf{C}}
defined by

Sh(\mathbf{c},U)(\mathbf{C}):=\mathop{\rm SL}\nolimits_{2}(F)\backslash{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})^{(\mathbf{c})}/(U\cap\mathop{\rm SL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})).

For any positive integer
n
, put

U_{N}(p^{n})=\left\{g=\begin{pmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{o}})\mid a,d\in 1+p^{n}N\widehat{\mathfrak{o}},\,c\in{\mathfrak{c}}p^{n}\widehat{\mathfrak{d}}_{F},\,b\in N\widehat{\mathfrak{d}}_{F}^{-1}\right\}.

Assume
N
is large enough such that

(3.1)

U_{N}(1)\text{ is neat and }\det(U_{N}(1))\cap\mathfrak{o}_{+}^{\times}\subset(U_{N}(1)\cap\mathfrak{o}^{\times})^{2}.

With the above condition, we see that the map

(3.2)

{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})^{(\mathbf{c})}\to\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n})(\mathbf{C}),\quad x=(\tau_{x},g_{x})\mapsto[(A_{x},\lambda_{x},\eta_{x},j_{x})]

induces an isomorphism

Sh(\mathbf{c},U_{N}(p^{n}))(\mathbf{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n})(\mathbf{C}).

3.3.
Hilbert modular forms

Geometric modular forms

Let
k
be an integer.
Let
R_{0}
be a
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
-algebra. Let
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};R_{0})
be the space of geometric Hilbert modular forms of weight
k\Sigma
and level
\Gamma(N)\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{n})
defined over
R_{0}
. Recall that an element
f\in\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};R_{0})
is a rule
f
which assigns a
{\mathfrak{c}}
-polarized quadruple
(A,\lambda,\eta,j)
of HBAV over a
R_{0}
-algebra
R
together with an
(\mathfrak{o}\otimes R)
-basis
\omega
of
{\rm H}^{0}(A,\Omega^{1}_{A/R})
(or equivalently an isomorphism
\omega^{*}:\mathop{\rm Lie}\nolimits(A_{x})\simeq\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes R
) an element
f(A,\lambda,\eta,j,\omega)\in R
such that

(M1)

the value
f(A,\lambda,\eta,j,\omega)
only depends on the isomorphism class of
(A,\lambda,\eta,j,\omega)
,

(M2)

f(A,\lambda,\eta,j,\alpha^{-1}\omega)=\alpha^{k\Sigma}\cdot f(A,\lambda,\eta,j;\omega)
for any
\alpha\in(\mathfrak{o}\otimes R)^{\times}
,

(M3)

if
u:R\to R^{\prime}
is a homomorphism of
R_{0}
-algebras, then

f((A,\lambda,\eta,j,\omega)\times_{R}R^{\prime})=u(f(A,\lambda,\eta,j,\omega)).

We put

\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};R_{0}).

We briefly recall the
q
-expansions of geometric modular forms. If
R
is any ring and
M
is a lattice in
F
, let
R\llbracket{M_{+}}\rrbracket
be the ring of all formal series

\sum_{\beta\in M_{+}\cup\left\{0\right\}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta},\quad a_{\beta}\in R.

We fix a set
\mathcal{C}=\left\{l_{1},\dots,l_{[F:\mathbf{Q}]}\right\}
consisting of
[F:\mathbf{Q}]
-linearly independent
\mathbf{Q}
-linear forms
l_{i}:F\to\mathbf{Q}
such that
l_{i}(F_{+})\subset\mathbf{Q}_{+}
. Define
M_{\mathcal{C}_{+}}=\left\{x\in M\mid l_{i}(x)\geq 0,\,l_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\right\}
. Denote by
R\llbracket{M,\mathcal{C}}\rrbracket
the ring of all formal series
\sum_{\beta\in M_{\mathcal{C}_{+}}}a_{\beta}q^{\beta}
. It is clear that
R\llbracket{M_{+}}\rrbracket\subset R\llbracket{M,\mathcal{C}}\rrbracket
for any choice of
\mathcal{C}
. Let

R((M,\mathcal{C})):=R\llbracket{M,\mathcal{C}}\rrbracket[U^{-1}],\quad U=\left\{q^{\beta}\mid\beta\in M_{+}\right\}.

Let
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})
be a pair of fractional ideals of
F
such that
{\mathfrak{c}}={\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1}
. To any geometric modular form
f\in\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})
, we can associate a power series
f|_{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}(q)\in R_{0}(({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}},\mathcal{C}))
obtained by evaluating
f
at the Tate HBAV
{\rm Tate}_{{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}}(q)
together with a canonical differential over
\mathbf{Z}[\zeta_{N}](({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}},\mathcal{C}))
(
cf.

[
Kat78
, 1.1 and (1.2.12)]
and
[
Gor02
, Chap 5, §2]
). This power series
f|_{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}
is called the
q
-expansion of
f
at the cusp
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})
.

Classical modular forms

Define the automorphy factor by

(3.3)

J:\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F\otimes\mathbf{R})\times{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\to(F\otimes\mathbf{C})^{\times},\quad J(\begin{pmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{pmatrix}):=c\tau+d.

For any idele
\mathbf{c}\in\widehat{F}^{{\times}}
and an open-compact subgroup
U\subset\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})
, we let
M_{k}(\mathbf{c},U)
denote the space of classical modular forms of weight
k\Sigma
and level
U
, which consists of holomorphic functions
\mathbf{f}:{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})^{(\mathbf{c})}\to\mathbf{C}
such that

(3.4)

\mathbf{f}(\alpha(\tau,g)u)=J(\alpha,\tau)^{k\Sigma}f(\tau,g)\text{ for }\alpha\in\mathop{\rm SL}\nolimits_{2}(F);\,u\in U\cap\mathop{\rm SL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F}).

Letting
\mathbf{c}
be the idele such that
\mathbf{c}\in\widehat{F}^{(pN){\times}}
with
\mathbf{c}\mathfrak{o}={\mathfrak{c}}
, we put

M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};\mathbf{C})=M_{k}(\mathbf{c},U_{N}(p^{n})).

Give a pair of prime-to-
pN
fractional ideals
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})
with
{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1}={\mathfrak{c}}
, choose
a,b\in\widehat{F}^{(pN){\times}}
with
a\mathfrak{o}={\mathfrak{a}}
and
b\mathfrak{o}={\mathfrak{b}}
. Each
\mathbf{f}\in M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};\mathbf{C})
admits the Fourier expansion

\mathbf{f}(\tau,\begin{pmatrix}{b^{-1}}&0\\
0&{a}\end{pmatrix})=c_{0}(\mathbf{f})+\sum_{\beta\in(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}})_{+}}c_{\beta}(\mathbf{f})e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(\beta\tau)},

where
\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits_{F/\mathbf{Q}}:F\otimes\mathbf{C}\to\mathbf{C}
. The
q
-expansion of
f
at the cusp
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})
is defined to be the power series

\mathbf{f}|_{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}(q)=c_{0}(\mathbf{f})+\sum_{\beta\in(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}})_{+}}c_{\beta}(\mathbf{f})q^{\beta}\in\mathbf{C}\llbracket{(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}})_{+}}\rrbracket.

If
R_{0}
is a
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
-algebra in
\mathbf{C}
, let

M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};R_{0})=\left\{\mathbf{f}\in M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};\mathbf{C})\mid\mathbf{f}|_{(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})}(q)\in R_{0}\llbracket{(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})_{+}}\rrbracket\right\}.

We put

M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})=\varinjlim_{n}M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n};R_{0}).

By
[
Rap78
, Lemme 6.12]
(or
[
Kat78
, (1.6.3)]
) and the
q
-expansion principle (
cf.

[
Rap78
, Theoreme 6.7]
and
[
Kat78
, (1.2.16)]
), we have the isomorphism

(3.5)

\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0}),\quad f\mapsto f^{\rm an}(x)=f(A_{x},\eta_{x},j_{x},2\pi\sqrt{-1}\omega_{x}^{\rm an}).

Here
\omega_{x}^{\rm an}
is given by the canonical isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Lie}\nolimits(A_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F\otimes\mathbf{C}=\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{C}
.

3.4.
The Igusa tower and
p
-adic modular forms

Let
R_{0}
be a
p
-adic
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
-algebra, i.e.
R_{0}
is a
\mathbf{Z}[\frac{1}{N\Delta_{F}},\zeta_{N}]
-algebra and
R_{0}=\varprojlim R_{0}/p^{m}R_{0}
. The Igusa tower
{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)_{/R_{0}}
is the formal scheme

{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)_{/R_{0}}:=\varinjlim_{m}\varprojlim_{n}\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{n})_{/R_{0}/p^{m}R_{0}}.

We let
\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathfrak{c}},N)
be the ordinary locus (i.e., the locus where the Hasse invariant is invertible) of the formal completion of
\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}},N,p^{0})_{/\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
along the special fiber. The natural projection
\pi_{{\rm Ig}}:{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)_{/\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\to\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}({\mathfrak{c}},N)
given by forgetting the
\Gamma_{1}(p^{\infty})
-level structure is Galois with group
(\mathfrak{o}\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p})^{\times}
.

The space
V({\mathfrak{c}},N,R_{0})
of
p
-adic modular forms of tame level
\Gamma(N)
is defined to be the ring of formal functions on the Igusa tower
{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)
. In other words,
V({\mathfrak{c}},N,R_{0})
is the ring of global sections of the structure sheaf of the formal scmee
{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)_{/R_{0}}
. To any
p
-adic modular form
f\in V({\mathfrak{c}},N,R_{0})
and
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})
fractional ideals with
{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1}={\mathfrak{c}}
, one can also associate a power series
f|_{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}
in the
p
-adic completion
R_{0}\widehat{(({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}},\mathcal{C}))}
by evaluating at the Tate HBAV
{\rm Tate}_{{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}}(q)
over
R_{0}\widehat{(({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}},\mathcal{C}))}
.

Let
R
be a
p
-adic
R_{0}
-algebra containing the Galois closure of
\mathfrak{o}
in
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. For any point
x=[(A_{x},\lambda_{x},\eta_{x},j_{x})]\in{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)(R)
, the
\Gamma_{1}(p^{\infty})
-level structure
j_{x}
induces a canonical isomorphism
\hat{j}_{x}:\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}{}_{/R}\to\widehat{A}_{x}
of formal groups, which in turn produces the isomorphism
(\hat{j}_{x})_{*}:\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathop{\rm Lie}\nolimits(A_{x})
of
(\mathfrak{o}\otimes R)
-modules by passage to Lie algebras. Define

(3.6)

\pi_{\rm HT}(x):=(\hat{j}_{x})_{*}^{-1}:\mathop{\rm Lie}\nolimits(A_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}\otimes R

to be the inverse of
(\hat{j}_{x})_{*}
(
cf.

[
Kat78
, (1.10.11)]
).

If
f\in\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})
is a geometric modular form of level
\Gamma_{1}(p^{\infty})
, we define the
p
-avatar
\widehat{f}\in V({\mathfrak{c}},N;R_{0})
is defined as follows for any
x\in{\rm Ig}({\mathfrak{c}},N)(R)
, define

(3.7)

\widehat{f}(x):=f(x,\pi_{\rm HT}(x))

which preserves the
q
-expansions
(
cf.

[
Kat78
, Theorem (1.10.15)]
).

3.5.
CM points

Let
K
be a totally imaginary quadratic extension of
F
. Let
\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}={\mathrm{N}}_{K/F}(\mathfrak{D}_{K/F})
be the relative discriminant of
K/F
. Suppose that
N
is prime to
\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}
. Decompose
N\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{N}^{+}\mathfrak{N}^{-}
, where
\mathfrak{N}^{+}
is a product of primes split in
K
and
\mathfrak{N}^{-}
is a product of primes inert in
K
.
By the approximation theorem, we can choose
\vartheta\in K
such that

(d1)

\overline{\vartheta}=-\vartheta
and

\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\sigma(\vartheta)>0
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
,

(d2)

{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{o}_{K}):=\mathfrak{d}_{F}^{-1}(2\vartheta\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}^{-1})
is prime to
pN\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}
.

Consider the isomorphism
q_{\vartheta}:V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}K
defined by
q_{\vartheta}(ae_{1}+be_{2})=a\vartheta+b
. Define the embedding
\iota:K\hookrightarrow\mathrm{M}_{2}(F)
by

\iota(a\vartheta+b)=\begin{pmatrix}{b}&{-\vartheta\overline{\vartheta}a}\\
{a}&{b}\end{pmatrix}.

Note that

(3.8)

q_{\vartheta}((0,1)\iota(\alpha))=\alpha\text{
and }q_{\vartheta}(x)\alpha=q_{\vartheta}(x\iota(\alpha))\text{ for $\alpha\in K$ and $x\in V$}.

The CM type
\Sigma
gives the isomorphism

\displaystyle i_{\Sigma}\colon K\otimes\mathbf{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F\otimes\mathbf{C}\simeq\mathbf{C}^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\alpha\in K\mapsto(\sigma(\alpha))_{\sigma\in\Sigma}.

Let
{\rm q}_{\vartheta_{\Sigma}}:=i_{\Sigma}\circ q_{\vartheta}:V\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F\otimes\mathbf{C}
with
\vartheta_{\Sigma}:=i_{\Sigma}(\vartheta)\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}
.

For a finite non-split place
v
of
K/F
, let
\left\{1,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{v}\right\}
be an
\mathfrak{o}_{v}
-basis of
\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}
. We further fix a decomposition
\mathfrak{N}^{+}={\mathfrak{F}}{{\mathfrak{F}}}_{c}
with
({\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{F}}_{c})=1
and
{\mathfrak{F}}\subset{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}^{c}
. If
v\mid p\mathfrak{N}^{+}
with
w\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{F}}
, i.e.
w\in\Sigma_{p}
or
w\mid{\mathfrak{F}}
, then we have
\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}=\mathfrak{o}_{v}e_{w}\oplus\mathfrak{o}_{v}e_{\overline{w}}
, where
e_{w}
and
e_{\overline{w}}
are the idempotents of
\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}
corresponding to
w
and
\overline{w}
respectively. Then
\left\{e_{w},e_{\overline{w}}\right\}
is an
\mathfrak{o}_{v}
-basis of
\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}
. In this case, we let
\vartheta_{w}\in F_{v}
such that
\vartheta=-\vartheta_{w}e_{\overline{w}}+\vartheta_{w}e_{w}
.
We shall also fix a finite idele
d_{F}=(d_{F_{v}})\in\widehat{F}
such that
d_{F}\mathfrak{o}=\mathfrak{d}_{F}
. By condition (d2), we choose

d_{F_{v}}=\frac{2\vartheta}{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{v}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{v}}}\text{ if }v\mid\mathfrak{d}_{K/F},d_{F_{v}}=2\vartheta_{v}\text{ if }v\mid\mathfrak{N}^{-}\text{ and }\quad d_{F_{v}}=-2\vartheta_{w}\text{ if }w\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{F}}.

Now we make a particular choice of a local basis
\left\{e_{1,v},e_{2,v}\right\}
of
K_{v}
over
F_{v}
for each finite
place
v
of
F
such that

\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}=\mathfrak{o}_{v}e_{1,v}\oplus\mathfrak{o}_{v}^{*}e_{2,v}
.

•

If
v\nmid pN
,
\left\{e_{1,v},e_{2,v}\right\}
is taken to be
\left\{\vartheta,1\right\}
for all but finitely many
v
.

•

If
v\mid\mathfrak{N}^{-}
is non-split, let

\left\{e_{1,v},e_{2,v}\right\}=\left\{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{v},d_{F_{v}}\cdot 1\right\}
.

•

If
v\mid p\mathfrak{N}^{+}
, let
\left\{e_{1,v},e_{2,v}\right\}=\left\{e_{\overline{w}},d_{F_{v}}\cdot e_{w}\right\}
with
w\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{F}}
.

Let
\varsigma_{v}
be
the element in
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F_{v})
defined by

(3.9)

q_{\vartheta}(e_{i}\varsigma_{v}^{-1})=e_{i,v}.

In view of our choices of
d_{F_{v}}
, one verifies that

\det\varsigma_{v}=1\iff\langle q_{\vartheta}^{-1}(e_{2,v}),q_{\vartheta}^{-1}(e_{1,v})\rangle=1\text{ for }v\mid pN.

In addition, the matrix representation of
\varsigma_{v}
according to
\left\{e_{1},e_{2}\right\}
for
v\mid p\mathfrak{N}^{+}
is given by

(3.10)

\displaystyle\varsigma_{v}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}{\frac{d_{F_{v}}}{2}}&{-{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
{\frac{-d_{F_{v}}}{2\vartheta_{w}}}&{\frac{-1}{2\vartheta_{w}}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}{-\vartheta_{w}}&{-{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
{1}&{\frac{-1}{2\vartheta_{w}}}\end{pmatrix}\text{ if }v=w\overline{w}\text{ and }w\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{F}}.

Let
{\bf h}
be the set of finite places of
F
, and put
\varsigma_{{{\bf h}}}=\prod_{v\in{{\bf h}}}\varsigma_{v}
. By definition, we have

(3.11)

q_{\vartheta}((\mathscr{L}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{Z}})\cdot\varsigma_{{{\bf h}}}^{-1})=\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{K}.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a fractional ideal of
K
coprime to
pN
and put

{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A})={\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{o}_{K}){\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak{A}).

Define the CM point

(3.12)

x(\mathfrak{A}):=[(\vartheta_{\Sigma},\iota(a)\varsigma_{\bf h})]=[(A_{\mathfrak{A}},\lambda_{\mathfrak{A}},\eta_{\mathfrak{A}},j_{\mathfrak{A}})]\in\mathfrak{M}({\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}),N)(\mathbf{C}),

where
a\in\widehat{K}^{(pN){\times}}
is an idele with
a\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{A}
. By the theory of CM abelian varieties, the triple
(A_{\mathfrak{A}},\lambda_{\mathfrak{A}},\eta_{\mathfrak{A}})
is defined over a number field
L
(
[
Shi98
, 18.6, 21.1]
), which in turn descends to a triple the triple
(A_{\mathfrak{A}},\lambda_{\mathfrak{A}},\eta_{\mathfrak{A}})
defined over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
by a theorem of Serre-Tate. On the other hand, since the CM type
\Sigma
is
p
-ordinary with respect to
\iota_{p}
, the reduction
A_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L},\iota_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
is an ordinary abelian variety (
cf.

[
Kat78
, 5.1.27]
), and hence the
\Gamma_{1}(p^{\infty})
-level structure
j_{\mathfrak{A}}
descends to a
\Gamma_{1}(p^{\infty})
-level structure over
\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\rm ur}
. We conclude that the CM point
x(\mathfrak{A})
can be descended to a
{\mathcal{W}}
-point
x(\mathfrak{A})
in the Igusa tower
{\rm Ig}(N,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}))({\mathcal{W}})
for some finite extension
{\mathcal{W}}
of
\widehat{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\rm ur}
(
[
Kat78
, 5.1.19-20]
).

4.
Eisenstein series and the improved Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions

4.1.
Notation and convention

Denote by
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\mathbf{Q}}=\prod\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{v}}:\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}/\mathbf{Q}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
the unique additive character with
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\mathbf{R}}(x)=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}x)
and by
{|\!\cdot\!|}_{\mathbf{A}_{Q}}=\prod{|\!\cdot\!|}_{\mathbf{Q}_{v}}:\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}/\mathbf{Q}^{\times}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
the absolute value so that
\left|\cdot\right|_{\mathbf{R}}
is the usual absolute value on
\mathbf{R}
. Let
E
be a number field. Let
\mathfrak{d}_{E}
be the different of
E/\mathbf{Q}
and
\Delta_{E}={\mathrm{N}}_{E/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{d}_{E})
be the absolute discriminant of
E
. Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{E}:=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\mathbf{Q}}\circ\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits_{E/\mathbf{Q}}:\mathbf{A}_{F}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
. Denote by
{|\!\cdot\!|}_{\mathbf{A}_{E}}={|\!\cdot\!|}_{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{E/\mathbf{Q}}:\mathbf{A}_{E}^{\times}\to\mathbf{R}_{+}
. If
a\in\mathbf{A}_{E}^{\times}
is an idele and
v
is a place of
E
, denote by
a_{v}\in E_{v}^{\times}
the component of
a
at
v
. If
\alpha\in E^{\times}
and
S
is a finite set of places of
E
, denote by
(\alpha)_{S}\in\mathbf{A}_{E}^{\times}
the idele whose
v
-component is
\alpha
if
v\in S
and
1
if
v\not\in S
.

L
-functions and epsilon factors

If
\chi:C_{E}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is an idele class character (or Hecke character) of
L^{\times}
unramified outside
S
. If
v
is a place of
E
, let
\chi_{v}:E_{v}^{\times}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the local component of
\chi
at
v
. Let
L(s,\chi_{v})
and
\varepsilon(s,\chi_{v})=\varepsilon(s,\chi_{v},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{E_{v}})
be the local
L
-factor and local epsilon factor of
\chi_{v}
in
[
Tat79
, 3.1]
. Let
L(s,\chi)=\prod_{v}L(s,\chi_{v})
be the complete
L
-function of
\chi
and
\varepsilon(s,\chi):=\prod_{v}\varepsilon(s,\chi_{v})
be the global epsilon factor
[
Tat79
, 3.5.1-2]
. Denote by
L_{\rm fin}(s,\chi):=\prod_{v<\infty}L(s,\chi_{v})
be the
L
-function without archimedean factors. If
\chi={\mathbf{1}}
is the trivial character of
C_{E}^{\times}
, then we put

\zeta_{E_{v}}(s)=L(s,{\mathbf{1}}_{v});\quad\zeta_{E}(s)=L(s,{\mathbf{1}}).

In particular,

\zeta_{\mathbf{R}}(s)=\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(s)=\pi^{-\frac{s}{2}}\Gamma(\frac{s}{2}),\quad\zeta_{\mathbf{C}}(s)=\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s).

and
\zeta_{\mathbf{Q}}(2)=\pi/6
with this definition.

Measures

Let
v
be a place of
E
. If
v
is non-archimedean, let
\mathrm{d}x_{v}
be the self-dual Haar measure on
\mathfrak{o}_{E_{v}}
with respect to the additive character
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{E_{v}}
, i.e.
\mathrm{d}x_{v}
is the measure such that
\mathop{\rm vol}\nolimits(\mathfrak{o}_{E_{v}},\mathrm{d}x_{v})=\left|\mathfrak{d}_{E}\right|_{E_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. If
v
is a real place, then
\mathrm{d}x_{v}
is the Lebesgue measure. If
v
is a complex place, then
\mathrm{d}x_{v}
is twice the Lebesgue measure. Let
\mathrm{d}^{\times}x_{v}
be the Haar measure on
E_{v}^{\times}
defined by
\mathrm{d}^{\times}x_{v}=\zeta_{F_{v}}(1)\left|x_{v}\right|_{E_{v}}^{-1}\mathrm{d}x_{v}
(so
\mathop{\rm vol}\nolimits(\mathfrak{o}_{E_{v}}^{\times},\mathrm{d}x^{\times}_{v})=\left|\mathfrak{d}_{E}\right|_{E_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
). The product measure
\mathrm{d}x=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{v}
is the Tamagawa measure on
\mathbf{A}_{E}
. Denote by
{\mathcal{S}}(E_{v})
the space of Bruhat-Schwartz functions on
E_{v}
. For
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}}(E_{v})
, the Fourier transform
\widehat{\phi}\in{\mathcal{S}}(E_{v})
is defined by

\widehat{\phi}(y)=\int_{E_{v}^{\times}}\phi(x_{v})\boldsymbol{\psi}_{E_{v}}(yx_{v})\mathrm{d}x_{v}.

If
\chi:E_{v}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is a continuous character, the Tate integral
Z(\phi,\chi,s)
is defined by

Z(\phi,\chi,s)=\int_{E_{v}^{\times}}\phi(x_{v})\chi(x_{v})\left|x_{v}\right|_{E_{v}}^{s}\mathrm{d}^{\times}x_{v}\quad(s\in\mathbf{C}).

Characters

We fix an isomorphism
\iota_{p}:\mathbf{C}\simeq\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
once and for all. Let
{\rm I}_{E}
be the set of all embeddings
E\to\mathbf{C}
. Each
k\in\mathbf{Z}[{\rm I}_{E}]
shall be regarded as a character
k:(E\otimes\mathbf{R})^{\times}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
and
(E\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{p})^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
via
\iota_{p}
.
If
\chi
is a
p
-adic character of
G_{E,S}
, we say
\chi
is locally algebraic of weight
k\in\mathbf{Z}[{\rm I}_{E}]
if
\chi(\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{E}(x))=x^{k}
for all
x\in(E\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{p})^{\times}
sufficiently close to
1
. We shall also view
\chi
as an idele class character via
\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{E}
and still denote by
\chi
if there is no fear for confusion. In particular, a primitive ray class character
\chi
modulo
{\mathfrak{c}}
shall be identified with an idele class character
\chi
of
E
of conductor
{\mathfrak{c}}
. If
\chi:C_{E}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is an idele class character of
F
of level
\mathfrak{n}
, we put

(4.1)

\chi(s\mathcal{O}_{E}):=\chi(s)\text{ for }s\in\widehat{E}^{(\mathfrak{n}){\times}}.

Therefore,

\chi(\mathfrak{q}):=\chi({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}})=\chi_{\mathfrak{q}}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}})\text{ for }\mathfrak{q}\not\in S.

To an idele class character
\chi:C_{E}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
of infinity type
k\in\mathbf{Z}[{\rm I}_{E}]
, i.e.
\chi_{\infty}(a_{\infty})=a_{\infty}^{k}
, we can associate a
p
-adic idele class character
\widehat{\chi}:C_{E}\to\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
defined by

(4.2)

\widehat{\chi}(a)=\chi(a)\iota_{p}(a_{\infty}^{-k})a_{p}^{k}.

We call
\widehat{\chi}
the
p
-adic avatar of
\chi
. The
p
-adic character
\widehat{\chi}
factors through
\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{E}:C_{E}\to G_{E}^{ab}
, and hence gives rise to a locally algebraic
p
-adic Galois character
\widehat{\chi}:G_{E}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
of weight
k
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\omega}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p})/\mathbf{Q})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the character such that
\iota_{p}\circ\boldsymbol{\omega}
is the
p
-adic Teichmüller character. Let
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{E}=\boldsymbol{\omega}|_{\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(E(\zeta_{p})/E)}
. Viewed as an idele class character, we have
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{E}=\boldsymbol{\omega}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{E/\mathbf{Q}}
.

4.2.
Generalities on Eisenstein series

Let
F
be a totally real number field as before. Denote by
{\bf h}
the set of finite places of
F
. We shall identify
\Sigma
with the set of infinite places of
F
. We recall a well-known construction of adelic Eisenstein series on
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})
in
[
Jac72
, §19]
(
cf.

[
Bum97
, p. 351]
). Let
(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
be a pair of Hecke characters of
F^{\times}
, i.e. quasi-characters of
\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash F^{\times}
. For each place
v
, let
I(\chi_{1,v},\chi_{2,v};s)
be the space of smooth (and
\mathop{\rm SO}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{R})
-finite if
v
is archimedean) functions
f:\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F_{v})\to\mathbf{C}
such that

f(\begin{pmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{0}&{d}\end{pmatrix}g)=\chi_{1}(a)\chi_{2}(d)\left|\frac{a}{d}\right|_{v}^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}f(g).

Here
{|\!\cdot\!|}_{v}={|\!\cdot\!|}_{F_{v}}
. Let
B
be the upper triangular subgroup of
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}
. Define the adelic Eisenstein series
E_{\mathbf{A}}(-,f):\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F)\backslash\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{C}
associated with
f
by

(4.3)

E_{\mathbf{A}}(g,f):=\sum_{\gamma\in B(F)\backslash\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F)}f(\gamma g),\quad g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F}),

provided the sum is convergent.
For
f=\otimes f_{v}\in I(\chi_{1},\chi_{2};s):=\otimes^{\prime}_{v}I(\chi_{1,v},\chi_{2,v};s)
and
\beta\in F^{\times}
, define the local Whittaker function
W_{\beta}(-,f_{v}):\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F_{v})\to\mathbf{C}
by

W_{\beta}(g_{v},f_{v})=\int_{F_{v}}f_{v}(\begin{pmatrix}{0}&{-1}\\
{1}&{0}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{1}&{x_{v}}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}g_{v})\boldsymbol{\psi}_{F_{v}}(-\beta x_{v})\mathrm{d}x_{v}.

The global Whittaker function is defined by

W_{\beta}(g,f)=\prod_{v}W_{\beta}(g_{v},f_{v}).

Then we have Fourier expansion

(4.4)

E_{\mathbf{A}}(g,f)=f(g)+Mf(g)+\sum_{\beta\in F^{\times}}W_{\beta}(g,f),

where
Mf(g)
is obtained by the analytic continuation of the intertwining integral

Mf(g)=\int_{\mathbf{A}_{F}}f(\begin{pmatrix}{0}&{-1}\\
{1}&{0}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{1}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}g)\mathrm{d}x,\,g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})

(
cf.

[
Bum97
, (7.15)]
).

4.3.
The construction of adelic Eisenstein series

Let
{\mathfrak{C}}
be an integral ideal of
K
such that

({\mathfrak{C}},p\mathfrak{d}_{K/F})=1.

Decompose
{\mathfrak{C}}={\mathfrak{C}}^{+}{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}
, where
{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}={\mathfrak{F}}{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}
is a product of split primes in

K/F
such that
({\mathfrak{F}},{\mathfrak{F}}_{c})=1
and
{\mathfrak{F}}\subset{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}^{c}
, and
{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}
is a product of inert primes in
K/F
. Let
\lambda
be a Hecke character of
K
of infinity type
k\Sigma
, namely

\lambda_{\infty}(z)=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\sigma(z)^{k}\text{ for }z\in K^{\times}.

Let
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\lambda}
be the conductor of
\lambda
. We assume that

{\mathfrak{C}}_{\lambda}={\mathfrak{C}}\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}\mathfrak{P}^{e_{\mathfrak{P}}}.

In particular,
\lambda
is unramified at primes in
\overline{\Sigma}_{p}
.

For each place
v
of
F
, we introduce some special Bruhat-Schwartz functions
\Phi_{\lambda,v}\in{\mathcal{S}}(F_{v}^{2})
as follows:

(1)

If
v\mid\infty
, then
\Phi_{\lambda,v}(x,y)=2^{-k}(x+\sqrt{-1}y)^{k}e^{-\pi(x^{2}+y^{2})}
.

(2)

If
v\nmid p{\mathfrak{C}}\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}}
is a finite place, let
\Phi_{\lambda,v}(x,y)=\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{v}}(x)\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{d}_{F_{v}}^{-1}}(y)
.

(3)

For
v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}
non-split in
K
, let

\Phi_{\lambda,v}(x,y):=\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}^{\times}}(xe_{1,v}+ye_{2,v})\lambda(xe_{1,v}+ye_{2,v})^{-1}

(4)

If
v\mid p{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}
with
v=w\overline{w}
and
w\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{F}}
, then

\Phi_{\lambda,v}(x,y)=\varphi_{\overline{w}}(x)\widehat{\varphi}_{w}(y),

where
\varphi_{w}(y)=\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{v}^{\times}}(y)\lambda_{w}(y)
and

\varphi_{\overline{w}}(x)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{v}}(x)&\text{ if }\overline{w}\nmid{\mathfrak{F}}_{c},\\
\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{v}^{\times}}(x)\lambda_{\overline{w}}^{-1}(x)&\text{ if }\overline{w}\mid{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}.\end{cases}

Let
\lambda_{+}:=\lambda|_{\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}}
be a Hecke character of
F^{\times}
. Define the function
f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}:\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F_{v})\to\mathbf{C}
by

(4.5)

f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}(g)=\left|\det g\right|_{v}^{s}\int_{F_{v}^{\times}}\Phi_{\lambda,v}((0,t_{v})g)\lambda_{+}(t_{v})\left|t_{v}\right|_{v}^{2s}\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{v}.

Let
f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}:=\otimes_{v}f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}\in I(\mathbf{1},\lambda^{-1}_{+};s-\frac{1}{2})
and define the Eisenstein series

E_{\lambda,s}:\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F)\backslash\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{C},\quad E_{\lambda,s}(g):=E_{\mathbf{A}}(g,f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}).

The analytic properties and the functional equation of this Eisenstein series have been studied in
[
Jac72
, §19]
. By the choice of our sections, we have

(4.6)

\displaystyle E_{\lambda,s}(\alpha gu_{\infty}z)=E_{\lambda,s}(g)J(u_{\infty},\sqrt{-1})^{-k\Sigma}\lambda_{+}^{-1}(z),

\displaystyle\alpha\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F),\,u_{\infty}\in\mathop{\rm SO}\nolimits_{2}(F\otimes\mathbf{R}),\,z\in\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}.

Here
J
is the automorphy factor in (
3.3
).

4.4.
The evaluation of Eisenstein series at CM points

Let
N
be a sufficiently large power of
{\mathrm{N}}_{K/\mathbf{Q}}({\mathfrak{C}})
so that the section
f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}
is invariant by
U_{N}(p^{n})
for some
n\gg 0
. Define the classical Eisenstein series
{\bf E}_{\lambda}:{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})\to\mathbf{C}
by

\displaystyle{\bf E}_{\lambda}(\tau,g_{{\bf h}})=

\displaystyle E_{\lambda,s}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix},g_{{\bf h}}\right)\bigg|_{s=0}\quad(\tau=x+\sqrt{-1}y).

Let
{\mathfrak{c}}
be an integral ideal of
F
with
({\mathfrak{c}},pN)=1
and
\mathbf{c}\in\widehat{F}^{(pN){\times}}
with
\mathbf{c}\mathfrak{o}={\mathfrak{c}}
.
From (
4.6
), we have

(4.7)

{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}:={\bf E}_{\lambda}|_{{\mathfrak{H}}_{F}\times\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\widehat{F})^{(\mathbf{c})}}\in M_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;\mathbf{C}).

We shall deduce an explicit Damerell’s formula for
{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
, which relates the CM values of Eisenstein series to Hecke
L
-values for CM fields. We begin with the period integral of adelic Eisenstein series. Let

\varsigma_{\infty}=(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\sigma(\vartheta)}&{0}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix})_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(F\otimes_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R});\quad\varsigma:=\varsigma_{\infty}\times\varsigma_{{{\bf h}}}\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{A}_{F}).

Define the toric period integral of Eisenstein series by

\ell_{K^{\times}}(E_{\lambda,s}):=\int_{K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times}}E_{\lambda,s}(\iota(z)\varsigma)\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z},

where
\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z}
is the quotient measure
\mathrm{d}^{\times}z/\mathrm{d}^{\times}t
.

Proposition 4.1
.

\ell_{K^{\times}}(E_{\lambda,s})=\left|\det(\varsigma)\right|_{\mathbf{A}}^{s}\frac{(\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}2^{s}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{K}}}L(s,\lambda)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{s-1})\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}(2\vartheta)\prod_{{\mathfrak{L}}\mid{\mathfrak{F}}}\frac{\lambda_{{\mathfrak{L}}}(2\vartheta)}{\varepsilon(s,\lambda_{\mathfrak{L}})}.

Proof.
.

By
[
Hsi12
, page 1530]

\ell_{K^{\times}}(E_{\lambda,s})=\prod_{v}\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v}),

where
\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})
is the local zeta integral

\displaystyle\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})=

\displaystyle\int_{K_{v}^{\times}/F_{v}^{\times}}f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}(\iota(z)\varsigma_{v})\lambda_{v}(z)\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\int_{K_{v}^{\times}}\Phi_{\lambda,v}((0,1)\iota(z)\varsigma_{v})\lambda_{v}(z)\left|z\overline{z}\right|_{v}^{s}\mathrm{d}^{\times}z.

In what follows, we fix a place
v
and write
z
for
z_{v}
.
For
v\mid\infty
,

\displaystyle\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}2\pi^{-1}(2^{-1}\sqrt{-1})^{k}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2(k+s)}e^{-\pi r^{2}}\mathrm{d}^{\times}r\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{k}\cdot 2^{s}\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(k+s).

If
v\nmid p{\mathfrak{C}}\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}}
, it follows from (
3.8
) and (
3.11
) that
\Phi_{\lambda,v}((0,1)\iota(z)\varsigma_{v})=\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}}(z)
, so we have

\displaystyle\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\int_{K_{v}^{\times}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}}(z)\lambda_{v}(z)\left|z\overline{z}\right|_{v}^{s}\mathrm{d}^{\times}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}L(s,\lambda_{v})\left|\mathfrak{d}_{K}\right|_{K_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If
v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}
, then

\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})=\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\int_{K_{v}^{\times}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{K_{v}}^{\times}}(z)\left|z\overline{z}\right|_{v}^{s}\mathrm{d}^{\times}z=\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\left|\mathfrak{d}_{K}\right|_{K_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

If
v\mid p{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}
with
v=w\overline{w}
and
w\mid{\mathfrak{F}}\Sigma_{p}
, then

\displaystyle\ell_{K_{v}^{\times}}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\lambda_{v})=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\lambda_{w}(-2\vartheta)\cdot Z(\widehat{\varphi}_{w},\lambda_{w},s)Z(\varphi_{\overline{w}},\lambda_{\overline{w}},s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\lambda_{w}(2\vartheta)\frac{L(s,\lambda_{w})}{L(1-s,\lambda_{w}^{-1})\varepsilon(s,\lambda_{w})}\cdot L(s,\lambda_{\overline{w}})\cdot\left|\det(\varsigma_{v})\right|_{v}^{s}\left|\mathfrak{d}_{K}\right|_{K_{v}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}.

In the last equality, we have used the local functional equation of the Tate integrals. Now the proposition follows from the above calculations of local zeta integrals.
∎

Let
\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}:=\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits(K)/\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits(F)
and let
h^{-}:=\#(\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K})
. We fix a set
\left\{\mathfrak{A}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{h^{-}}\right\}
of representatives for ideals
\mathfrak{A}_{i}
prime to
p{\mathfrak{C}}\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}}\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}
. Let

Q=[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:W\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}],

where
W
is the torsion subgroup of
K^{\times}
. Note that
Q=1
or
2
.

Corollary 4.2

(Damerell’s formula)
.

Let
\left\{x(\mathfrak{A}_{1}),\dots x(\mathfrak{A}_{h^{-}})\right\}
be the CM points defined in Definition
3.1
. Then have

\displaystyle\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))\lambda(\mathfrak{A}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}](\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}}{2^{d-1}Q\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}L(0,\lambda)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1})\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}(2\vartheta)\cdot\prod_{{\mathfrak{L}}\mid{\mathfrak{F}}}\frac{\lambda_{{\mathfrak{L}}}(2\vartheta)}{\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{\mathfrak{L}})}.

Proof.
.

By definition,
x(\mathfrak{A}_{i})=[(\vartheta_{\Sigma},\iota(a_{i})\varsigma_{\bf h})]
, where
a_{i}\in\widehat{K}^{(p{\mathfrak{C}}){\times}}
is an idele with
a_{i}\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{A}_{i}
. Let
U_{K}:=(K\otimes\mathbf{R})^{\times}\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{K}^{\times}
. There is an canonical isomorphism

K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times}/U_{K}\simeq\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K},\quad a\mapsto a\mathfrak{o}_{K}.

We have
