\displaystyle\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))\lambda(\mathfrak{A}_{i})=

\displaystyle\sum_{[a_{i}]\in K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times}/U_{K}}E_{\lambda,s}(\iota(a_{i})\varsigma)\lambda(a_{i})|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell_{K^{\times}}(E_{\lambda,s})|_{s=0}\cdot\frac{\#(\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K})}{\mathop{\rm vol}\nolimits(K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times},\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z})}.

We have
\mathop{\rm vol}\nolimits(K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times},\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z})=2L(1,\tau_{K/F})
.
From the classical formulae

(4.8)

\#\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}=\frac{2h_{K/F}}{Q};\quad L(1,\tau_{K/F})\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{K}}{\Delta_{F}}}=\frac{2^{d-1}h_{K/F}}{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}]},

where
h_{K/F}=h_{K}/h_{F}
is the relative class number, we obtain

(4.9)

\frac{\#(\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K})}{\mathop{\rm vol}\nolimits(K^{\times}\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}\backslash\mathbf{A}_{K}^{\times},\mathrm{d}^{\times}\overline{z})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{K}}}=\frac{\#(W)}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}=\frac{2}{Q}\cdot\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}]}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}.

The assertion follows from Proposition
4.1
and (
4.9
).
∎

4.5.
The evaluation of
p
-adic Eisenstein series at CM points

We next consider the CM values of
p
-adic Eisenstein series constructed from
{\bf E}_{\lambda,\mathbf{c}}
in (
4.7
).

Proposition 4.3
.

The
q
-expansion of
{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
at the cusp
(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})
is given by

{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}|_{(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})}(q)=c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})+\sum_{\beta\in(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})_{+}}c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})q^{\beta},

where
c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}(1)|_{s=0}
and

c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=({\mathrm{N}}\beta)^{k-1}\prod_{v\in{\bf h}}W_{\beta}\left(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}_{v}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)\bigg|_{s=0}.

Proof.
.

The is a standard argument. Let
f=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}
. By (
4.4
),
{\bf E}_{\lambda}(\tau,\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix})
is given by the series

\displaystyle f\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)|_{s=0}+Mf\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)|_{s=0}+\sum_{\beta\in F^{\times}}W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f\right)\Big|_{s=0},

where
\tau=x+\sqrt{-1}y
. Since
E_{\lambda}
is right invariant by
U_{N}(p^{n})
, we find that
W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f\right)=0
if
\beta\not\in N^{-1}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1}
by the equation
W_{\beta}(\begin{pmatrix}{1}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}g,f)=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{F}(\beta x)W_{\beta}(g,f)
. By the factorization of Whittaker functions, we obtain

W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f\right)|_{s=0}=c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})({\mathrm{N}}\beta)^{1-k}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(\beta x)}\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma}W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y_{\sigma}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f_{\Phi_{\lambda,\sigma},s}\right)|_{s=0}.

For
\sigma\in\Sigma
, an elementary calculation shows that

\displaystyle W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y_{\sigma}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f_{\Phi_{\lambda,\sigma},s}\right)|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=\Gamma(k)(-2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-k}y_{\sigma}^{1-k}\int_{\mathbf{R}}\frac{e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sigma(\beta)y_{\sigma}z}}{(z+\sqrt{-1})^{k}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\sigma(\beta)^{k-1}e^{-2\pi\sigma(\beta)y_{\sigma}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathbf{R}_{+}}(\sigma(\beta))

by Cauchy’s integration formula. We thus obtain

W_{\beta}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{y}&{x}\\
{0}&{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix},f\right)|_{s=0}=c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(\beta\tau)}\mathbb{I}_{F_{+}}(\beta).

On the other hand, the constant term is given by

c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)|_{s=0}+Mf_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)|_{s=0}.

If
v
is infinite, then
f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}|_{s=0}
belongs to the irreducible sub-representation in the induced representation
I({\mathbf{1}},\mathop{\rm sgn}\nolimits^{k};\frac{k-1}{2})\otimes\left|\det\right|^{-\frac{k}{2}}
, and hence
Mf_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}|_{s=0}=0
. We thus obtain

c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}}&0\\
0&{1}\end{pmatrix}\right)|_{s=0}=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}(1)|_{s=0}.

The assertion follows.
∎

Define the fudge factor

(4.10)

{\rm Fdg}(\lambda):=\prod_{v\mid{\mathfrak{F}}\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}}}\frac{\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{L(1,\lambda_{+,v}^{-1})}\prod_{v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}}\frac{\lambda_{v}(-d_{F_{v}}^{-1})\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{+,v})}{L(0,\lambda_{+,v})}\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}=\mathfrak{o}_{K},\\
0&\text{ if }{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}\neq\mathfrak{o}_{K}.\end{cases}

By definition,
{\rm Fdg}(\lambda)=1
if
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
is the unit ideal.

Lemma 4.4
.

We have

c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})={\rm Fdg}(\lambda)\cdot\frac{1}{2^{d}}L_{\rm fin}(1,\lambda_{+}^{-1})\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\mid p}(1-\lambda_{+}({\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}).

Proof.
.

If
{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}\neq\mathfrak{o}_{K}
, then
\Phi_{\lambda,v}(0,t)=0
and hence
f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}(1)=0
for
v\mid{\mathfrak{F}}\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}}
. Now we suppose that
{\mathfrak{F}}_{c}=\mathfrak{o}_{K}
. A direct calculation shows that

f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}(1)=\begin{cases}2^{-k}\sqrt{-1}^{k}\Gamma(s+k)\pi^{-(s+k)}=2^{-1}2^{s}(\sqrt{-1})^{k}\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(s+k)&\text{ if }v\mid\infty,\\
L(2s,\lambda_{+})\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{ if }v\nmid p{\mathfrak{C}}\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}},\\
\lambda_{v}^{-1}(d_{F_{v}})\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{ if }v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-},\\
\lambda_{+,v}(-1)\gamma(2s,\lambda_{+,v})^{-1}\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{ if }v\mid p{\mathfrak{F}}\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}}.\end{cases}

Here
\gamma(2s,\lambda_{+,v})=\frac{L(1-2s,\lambda_{+,v}^{-1})}{L(2s,\lambda_{+,v})}\varepsilon(2s,\lambda_{+,v})
is the
\gamma
-factor of
\lambda_{+,v}
. We have

L(s,\lambda_{+})=\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(s+k+\epsilon)^{[F:\mathbf{Q}]}\cdot L_{\rm fin}(s,\lambda_{+}),

where
\epsilon=0
if
k
is even and
\epsilon=1
if
k
is odd. By the functional equation of
L(s,\lambda_{+})
and
\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{+,v})=\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}
for
v\nmid p{\mathfrak{C}}\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}}
, we find that
c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=f_{\Phi_{\lambda},s}(1)|_{s=0}
equals

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{v}f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}(1)\right)\big|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}^{k+\epsilon}\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(k)}{2\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(k+\epsilon)}\right)^{d}\prod_{v\mid p{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}}\frac{\lambda_{+,v}(-1)\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{L(1,\lambda_{+,v}^{-1})}\prod_{v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}}\frac{\lambda_{v}(d_{F_{v}}^{-1})\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{+,v})}{L(0,\lambda_{+,v})}\cdot\frac{L(2s,\lambda_{+})|_{s=0}}{\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{+})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}^{k+\epsilon}\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(k)\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(1-k+\epsilon)}{2\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(k+\epsilon)}\right)^{d}\prod_{v\mid\infty}\lambda_{v}(-1){\rm Fdg}(\lambda)\cdot L_{\rm fin}(1,\lambda_{+}^{-1})\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\mid p}(1-\lambda_{+}^{-1}({\mathfrak{p}}){\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1})

Now by reflection and duplicate formula for Gamma functions, we see that

\frac{\sqrt{-1}^{k+\epsilon}\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(k)\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(1-k+\epsilon)}{\Gamma_{\mathbf{R}}(k+\epsilon)}=(-1)^{k}.

Now the lemma follows.
∎

We write
\lambda_{+}=\chi_{+}{|\!\cdot\!|}^{k}
, where
\chi_{+}
is a ray class character of
F
modulo
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\lambda}\cap F
. Let
F_{\chi_{+}}
be the finite extension of
F
cut out by
\chi_{+}
. We say
\lambda_{+}
is of type
W
if
F_{\chi_{+}}
is contained in the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
F_{\infty}
of
F
. Take any prime
\mathfrak{q}_{0}\mid p{\mathfrak{C}}
of
\mathfrak{o}
such that its Frobenious
{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}
generates
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)
. Let
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)
be defined as
1-\lambda_{+}(\mathfrak{q}_{0})=1-\chi_{+}(\mathfrak{q}_{0}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{q}_{0}^{-k}\neq 0
if
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
and
\lambda_{+}
is of type
W
and
{\rm H}_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)=1
otherwise. Note that
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)
is a non-zero algberiac number.

Corollary 4.5
.

We have

H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)\cdot{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}|_{(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})}\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}\llbracket{(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})_{+}}\rrbracket.

In particular,
{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
descends to a geometric modular form
{\mathbb{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathfrak{c}},N;\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Q}
.

Proof.
.

For
\beta\in F^{\times}
, we put

{\bf a}_{\beta}^{(p)}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}}):=\prod_{v\nmid p\infty}W_{\beta}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{c}_{v}}&{}\\
{}&{1}\end{pmatrix})\bigg|_{s=0}.

For each place
v\nmid p
, we see that
\left|\mathfrak{d}_{F}\right|_{v}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s}
is the function
\phi_{\lambda,s,v}
defined in
[
Hsi14b
, §4.5]
. The explicit formula of
{\bf a}_{\beta}^{(p)}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})
can be easily deduced from
[
Hsi14b
, (4.9)]
, which in particular shows that
{\bf a}^{(p)}_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})
belongs to
\mathfrak{o}_{L}
for some number field
L
unramified outside
p
. On the other hand, for each
v\mid p
,

W_{\beta}(f_{\Phi_{\lambda,v},s},1)=\lambda_{+,v}(\beta)\left|\beta\right|_{F_{v}}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}}(\beta).

Let
\widehat{\lambda}_{+}
be the
p
-adic avatar of
\lambda_{+}
. So
\widehat{\lambda}_{+}((\beta)_{p})=\lambda_{+}((\beta)_{p})\beta^{k\Sigma}
for
\beta\in F^{\times}
. Therefore, from Proposition
4.3
, we deduce that

(4.11)

c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})={\bf a}^{(p)}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})\widehat{\lambda}_{+}((\beta)_{p})\cdot{\mathrm{N}}_{F/\mathbf{Q}}(\beta)^{-1}\left|{\mathrm{N}}_{F/\mathbf{Q}}(\beta)\right|_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{\mathfrak{o}_{F}\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(\beta)\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}.

By Lemma
4.4
, we see that
c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})=f_{\Phi_{\lambda,s}}(1)|_{s=0}
is give by a product of the value
L_{p}(s,\chi_{+}^{-1})
of the Deligne-Ribet
p
-adic
L
-function at
s=1-k
and the Fudge factor
{\rm Fdg}(\lambda)\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}
. It is well-known that
L_{p}(1-k,\chi_{+}^{-1})\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}
if
\chi_{+}
is not of type
W
and in general
(1-\lambda_{+}(\mathfrak{q}))\cdot L_{p}(1-k,\chi_{+}^{-1})\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}
for any
\mathfrak{q}
away from the conductor of
\chi_{+}
. It follows that the product
{\rm Fdg}(\lambda)L_{p}(1-k,\chi_{+}^{-1})\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}
unless
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
and
\chi_{+}
is of type
W
, and hence
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)\cdot c_{0}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}
.
∎

We let

{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}:=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}\in V({\mathfrak{c}},N;{\mathcal{W}})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}

be the
p
-adic avatar of
{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
defined in (
3.7
). Let
(\Omega_{p},\Omega_{\infty})\in(\mathfrak{o}_{F}\otimes{\mathcal{W}})^{\times}\times(F\otimes\mathbf{C})^{\times}
be the canonical
p
-adic and complex period associated with the polarized CM abelian variety
(F\otimes\mathbf{C})/i_{\Sigma}(\mathfrak{o}_{K})

[
HT94
, (4.4 a,b)]
. It will be convenient to use the modified complex period defined by

(4.12)

\Omega:=\Omega_{\infty}(1\otimes\pi)^{-1}(-\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\vartheta_{\Sigma})\in(F\otimes\mathbf{C})^{\times}.

Proposition 4.6
.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma}}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}\lambda(\mathfrak{A}_{i}){\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))=

\displaystyle\frac{2\widehat{\lambda}((2\vartheta)_{\Sigma_{p}})}{Q}\prod_{{\mathfrak{L}}\mid{\mathfrak{F}}}\frac{\lambda_{\mathfrak{L}}(2\vartheta)}{\varepsilon(0,\lambda_{\mathfrak{L}})}\cdot\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}]}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{(\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}}\cdot\frac{L(0,\lambda)}{\Omega^{k\Sigma}}\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1}).

Proof.
.

By
[
HT93
, Theorem 1.2]
, we have

\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma}}=\frac{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{k\Sigma}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))=\frac{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{k\Sigma}}{\bf E}_{\lambda}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i})),

so we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma}}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}\lambda(\mathfrak{A}_{i}){\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{k\Sigma}}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}\lambda(\mathfrak{A}_{i}){\bf E}_{\lambda}(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i})).

Now the assertion follows immediately from Corollary
4.2
together with the equation
\widehat{\lambda}((2\vartheta)_{\Sigma_{p}})=\lambda((2\vartheta)_{\Sigma_{p}})(2\vartheta)^{k\Sigma}
and the definition of the modified period
\Omega
in (
4.12
).
∎

4.6.
The improved Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions for CM fields

We let
G_{\infty}({\mathfrak{C}})
denote the Galois group
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K({\mathfrak{C}}p^{\infty})/K)
of the ray class field
K({\mathfrak{C}}p^{\infty})/K
modulo
{\mathfrak{C}}p^{\infty}
. Recall that Katz (
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
) and Hida-Tilouine (
{\mathfrak{C}}\neq(1)
) constructed an element
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}\in{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{G_{\infty}({\mathfrak{C}})}\rrbracket
, which is uniquely characterized by the following interpolation formula:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma})}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma+2j}}=

\displaystyle\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}]}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{k\Sigma}(2\pi)^{k+2j}}{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits(2\vartheta)^{j}\Omega_{\infty}^{k\Sigma+2j}}\cdot\lambda((2\vartheta)_{\Sigma_{p}})

\displaystyle\times{\rm E}_{p}(\lambda)L(0,\lambda)\prod_{\mathfrak{l}\mid{\mathfrak{C}}}(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{l})),

where

•

\widehat{\lambda}:G_{\infty}({\mathfrak{C}})\to{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
is a locally algebraic character of weight
k\Sigma+j(1-c)

critical
at
0
, namely either
k>0
and
j\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}[\Sigma]
or
k\leq 1
and
k\Sigma+j\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}[\Sigma]
,

•

{\rm E}_{p}(\lambda)
is the modified Euler factor defined by

{\rm E}_{p}(\lambda)=\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}\frac{(1-\lambda(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}))(1-\lambda(\mathfrak{P}^{-1}){\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{P}^{-1})}{\varepsilon(0,\lambda,\boldsymbol{\psi}_{K_{\mathfrak{P}}})},

•

L(s,\lambda)
is the complete Hecke
L
-function associated with
\lambda
defined by

L(s,\lambda):=\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(s+k\Sigma+j)L_{\rm fin}(s,\lambda)

(
cf.

[
HT93
, Theorem II]
). Put

\sigma_{2\vartheta}=\mathop{\rm rec}\nolimits_{K}((2\vartheta)_{\Sigma_{p}})\in G_{\infty}({\mathfrak{C}}).

Let
\chi
be a ray class character of
K
of conductor
{\mathfrak{C}}
. We let

\pi_{\chi}:{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{G_{\infty}({\mathfrak{C}})}\rrbracket\to{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{G_{\infty}(1)}\rrbracket

be the homomorphism defined by
\gamma\mapsto\chi(\gamma)[\gamma|_{K(p^{\infty})}]
and define the
p
-adic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\in{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{G_{\infty}(1)}\rrbracket
associated with the branch character
\chi
by

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi):=\pi_{\chi}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}\cdot[\sigma_{2\vartheta}]^{-1})\in{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{G_{\infty}(1)}\rrbracket.

The interpolation formula (
4.13
) can be rephrased as

(4.14)

\frac{\widehat{\nu}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi))}{\Omega_{p}^{k\Sigma+2j}}=\frac{[\mathfrak{o}_{K}^{\times}:\mathfrak{o}_{F}^{\times}]}{2^{d}\sqrt{\Delta_{F}}}\cdot\frac{1}{(\sqrt{-1})^{k\Sigma+j}}\cdot{\rm E}_{p}(\chi\nu)\cdot\frac{L(0,\chi\nu)}{\Omega^{k\Sigma+2j}},

where
\widehat{\nu}:G_{\infty}(1)\to{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
is a locally algebraic character of weight
k\Sigma+j(1-c)
critical at
0
and
\Omega
is the modified period in (
4.12
). This form is in accordance with the conjectural shape of the
p
-adic
L
-functions for motives
[
Coa89
, Conjecture A]
. Let
\Gamma:=\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)
. Put

\mathfrak{X}^{\rm alg}:=\left\{\widehat{\nu}:\Gamma\to{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}\mid\widehat{\nu}\text{ is locally algebraic of weight }k\Sigma,\,k\geq 2\right\}.

Proposition 4.7
.

There exists a unique
p
-adic measure
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}
on
\Gamma
valued in
V(N,{\mathfrak{c}};{\mathcal{W}})
such that

\int_{\Gamma}\widehat{\nu}(\sigma)\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(\sigma)=H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\chi\nu)\cdot{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi\nu,{\mathfrak{c}}}

for all
\widehat{\nu}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm alg}
.

Proof.
.

This is shown by a standard argument. First we note that since
\chi
is assumed to be unramified outside
p
,
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\chi\nu)=1-\chi(\mathfrak{q}_{0})\widehat{\nu}(\mathfrak{q}_{0})
if
\chi_{+}=1
and
1
otherwise. The interpolation of the constant term
\widehat{\nu}\mapsto H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\chi\nu)\cdot c_{0}({\bf E}_{\chi\nu},{\mathfrak{c}})
is a direct consequence of the existence of Deligne-Ribet
p
-adic
L
-function. For
\beta\in F^{\times}
, it is shown in
[
Hsi14b
, (4.13)]
that

\displaystyle H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\lambda)\cdot c_{\beta}({\bf E}_{\lambda},{\mathfrak{c}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}b_{j}\cdot\widehat{\lambda}(a_{j})\text{ for }b_{j}\in{\mathcal{W}},

\displaystyle a_{j}\in\widehat{F}^{(p{\mathfrak{F}}){\times}}\prod_{v\mid p{\mathfrak{F}}\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}}}\mathfrak{o}_{v}\prod_{v\mid{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}}K_{v}^{\times}\quad(\lambda=\chi\nu).

Here
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
depend on
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\beta
but do not rely on
\lambda
. Therefore, there exists a
{\mathcal{W}}
-valued measure on
\Gamma
interpolating
\beta
-th Fourier coefficient
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\chi\nu)\cdot c_{\beta}({\mathbb{E}}_{\chi\nu},{\mathfrak{c}})
. Since
{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
share the same
q
-expansion with
{\bf E}_{\lambda,{\mathfrak{c}}}
at
(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})
, we thus obtain a
p
-adic measure
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(q)
on
\Gamma
valued in the ring
{\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{(N^{-1}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})_{+}}\rrbracket
such that the value of
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(q)
at
\widehat{\nu}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm alg}
is the
q
-expansion of
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}(\chi\nu)\cdot{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi\nu,{\mathfrak{c}}}
. Since the set
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm alg}
is dense in the space of continuous functions on
\Gamma
, we deduce that the masure
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(q)
descends to a unique measure
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}
valued in
V({\mathfrak{c}},N;{\mathcal{W}})
such that the
q
-expansion of
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}
at the cusp
(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}^{-1})
is
\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(q)
from the irreducibility of the Igusa tower
{\rm Ig}(N,{\mathfrak{c}})
due to Ribet.
∎

Let
\mathscr{V}:G_{F}^{\rm ab}\to G_{K}^{\rm ab}
be the transfer map. Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)\to{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
be a
p
-adic character such that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}\circ\mathscr{V}|_{G_{K}}=\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>
. Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\overline{\Sigma}_{p}}(\sigma):=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(c\sigma c^{-1})
. Define the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions.

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,t,\chi):=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\overline{\Sigma}}^{t}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(\chi)\right),\quad(s,t)\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}.

Let
\psi
be the idele class character of
K
such that
\widehat{\psi}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}
.
For
s\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}
, put

{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(s):=\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\sigma)^{s}\mathrm{d}\mu^{\rm Eis}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(\sigma)\cdot\begin{cases}\frac{1}{1-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\mathfrak{q}_{0})^{s}}&\text{ if }{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)\text{ and }\chi_{+}={\mathbf{1}},\\
1&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}.

Then Proposition
4.6
and (
4.14
) implies that

(4.15)

\displaystyle\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(s)(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))\chi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{\chi}\cdot\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\sigma_{2\vartheta}\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{F}}^{-1})\cdot\frac{{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}))},

where

C_{\chi}:=\frac{2\chi(\sigma_{2\vartheta})}{Q}\prod_{{\mathfrak{L}}\mid{\mathfrak{F}}}\frac{\chi_{\mathfrak{L}}(2\vartheta)}{\varepsilon(0,\chi_{{\mathfrak{L}}})}\in\overline{\mathbf{Z}}_{(p)}^{\times}.

The following is a special case of
p
-adic Kronecker limit formula.

Proposition 4.8
.

Suppose that (

F

-Leo
) holds. If
\chi
is unramified everywhere and
\chi_{+}={\mathbf{1}}
, then we have

\frac{2^{d}{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})^{s})}\bigg|_{s=0}=\begin{cases}h_{K/F}&\text{ if }\chi={\mathbf{1}},\\
0&\text{ if }\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}.\end{cases}

Proof.
.

We have
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
since
\chi
is unramified everywhere. Put

{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}^{*}(s):={\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}(s)\cdot\frac{2^{d}}{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)}.

Note that
{\rm Fdg}(\lambda)=1
if
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
and hence
{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}}^{*}(s)|_{s=0}=1
is the constant function by a result of Colmez
[
Col88
]
. By (
4.15
), we have

\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}{\mathcal{E}}^{*}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})}(s)(x(\mathfrak{A}_{i}))\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\mathfrak{A}_{i})\chi(\mathfrak{A}_{i})=\frac{2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\sigma_{2\vartheta})^{s}}{Q}\cdot\frac{2^{d}{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})^{s})}

Evaluating at
s=0
of the above equation, we find that

\frac{2}{Q}\cdot\frac{2^{d}{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})^{s})}\bigg|_{s=0}=\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}\chi(\mathfrak{A}_{i})=\begin{cases}\#(\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K})&\text{ if }\chi={\mathbf{1}},\\
0&\text{ if }\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}.\end{cases}

It follows from the formula
\#(\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K})=2h_{K/F}/Q
.
∎

Theorem 4.9
.

Suppose that (

F

-Leo
) holds. If
\chi\neq{\mathbf{1}}
, then there exists a analytic
L
-function
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi)\in\Lambda_{K}
such that

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi))\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\chi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})).

We call
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(s,\chi):=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi))
the improved
p
-adic
L
-function.

Proof.
.

For any
[\mathfrak{A}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}
, let
\mathrm{d}\mu_{{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathfrak{A}}}
be the bounded
p
-adic measure on
\Gamma
obtained by evaluating
\mathrm{d}\mu_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\chi,{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A})}}
at the CM point
x(\mathfrak{A})
. Namely,
\mathrm{d}\mu_{{\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}}}
is characterized by the interpolation property

\int_{\Gamma}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\sigma)^{k}\mathrm{d}\mu_{{\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}}}(\sigma)={\mathcal{E}}_{\chi\psi^{k},{\mathfrak{c}}(\mathfrak{A})}(x(\mathfrak{A})).

Let
{\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}}\in\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits(\Lambda_{K})
be the corresponding element in the Iwasawa algebra of
\Gamma
. Then
{\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}}\in\Lambda_{K}
unless
\chi_{+}=1
and
{\mathfrak{C}}=(1)
.

(1-[\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}]){\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}}\in\Lambda_{K}
in general. Define

(4.16)

{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi):=\frac{[\sigma_{2\vartheta}^{-1}\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{F}}]}{C_{\chi}}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}_{i}]\in\mathop{\rm Cl}\nolimits^{-}_{K}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\chi,\mathfrak{A}_{i}}\cdot\chi(\mathfrak{A}_{i})[\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}_{i}}]\in\Lambda_{K}.

One verifies that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi)
satisfies the desired interpolation property by (
4.15
) and that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}^{*}(\chi)\in\Lambda_{K}
if
\chi\neq 1
by Proposition
4.8
.
∎

5.
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg method for Hilbert modular forms

5.1.
Hida families of Hilbert modular cusp forms

Let
M_{k}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi)
be the space of parallel weight
k\geq 1
Hilbert modular forms
of level
\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{n}p)
and Nebentypus
\xi
, where
\mathfrak{n}
is a prime to
p
ideal of
\mathfrak{o}_{F}
and
\xi
is a finite order idele class character modulo
\mathfrak{n}p
. We fix a set
\{{\mathfrak{c}}_{i}\}_{i=1,\ldots,h_{+}}
of the representatives of the narrow class group of
F
. For
\mathbf{f}\in M_{k}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
, we let

D(s,\mathbf{f})=\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}\text{: ideals of }\mathfrak{o}}C({\mathfrak{a}},\mathbf{f}){\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{-s}

be the associated Dirichlet series and let
C_{i}(0,\mathbf{f})
be the constant terms at the cusp
(\mathfrak{o},{\mathfrak{c}}_{i}^{-1})
(
[
Shi78
, (2.25)]
). The value
C({\mathfrak{a}},\mathbf{f})
is called the
{\mathfrak{a}}
-th Fourier coefficient of
\mathbf{f}
.

Let
F_{\infty}=F\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}
be the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
F
and let
\Lambda:={\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)}\rrbracket
be the Iwasawa algebra over
{\mathcal{W}}
associated with
F_{\infty}/F
. We shall assume that
p
is unramified in
F
throughout. Let
\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the
p
-adic cyclotomic character given by the composition
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)\to\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}_{\infty}/\mathbf{Q})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\mathbf{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbf{Q})\overset{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}{\simeq}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and for any integer
k
, let
\nu_{k}:\Lambda\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}
be the natural morphism induced by
\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>^{k}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Let
h_{+}
be the narrow class number of
F
. A
\Lambda
-adic modular form
\mathcal{F}
of level
N
and Nebentypus
\xi
is a collection of elements

\{C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{F}}),\,i=1,\dots,h_{+};\quad C({\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{F}),\text{ ${\mathfrak{a}}\subset\mathfrak{o}$ non-zero ideals}\}\subset\Lambda

such that for all but finitely many
k\geq 1
, there is a unique
\nu_{k}({\mathcal{F}})\in M_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k})
whose associated Dirichlet series is

D(s,\nu_{k}({\mathcal{F}}))=\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\nu_{k}(C({\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{F})){\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{-s}

and whose constant terms are given by
C_{i}(0,\nu_{k}({\mathcal{F}}))=\nu_{k}(C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{F}}))
. We denote by
{\bf M}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
the space of
\Lambda
-adic modular forms of level
\mathfrak{n}
and Nebentypus
\xi
and by
{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
the subspace of
\Lambda
-adic cusp forms. Namely,
{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
is the subspace of
\mathbf{M}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
consisting of
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathbf{M}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
with
\nu_{k}({\mathcal{F}})
is always a cusp form for all but finitely many
k\geq 1
. In particular, if
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
, then
C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{F}})=0
for all
i=1,\dots,h_{+}
.

We have a natural action of the Hecke operators
\{T_{\mathfrak{q}},U_{\mathfrak{l}}\}_{\mathfrak{q}\nmid\mathfrak{n}p,\mathfrak{l}\mid\mathfrak{n}p}
on
{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
, which are compatible with the Hecke action on
S_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k})
under the specialization
{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)\to S_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k})
along
\nu_{k}
. For any integral ideal
{\mathfrak{b}}
and
\mathcal{F}\in{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
, define
V_{\mathfrak{b}}\mathcal{F}\in{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n}{\mathfrak{b}},\xi)
by
C({\mathfrak{a}},V_{\mathfrak{b}}\mathcal{F})=C({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1},\mathcal{F})
. Hida defined an ordinary projector
e^{{\mathrm{ord}}}:=\underset{r\to\infty}{\lim}(\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}\mid p}U_{{\mathfrak{p}}})^{r!}
on
{\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
. The
\Lambda
-module
{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)=e^{{\mathrm{ord}}}({\bf S}(\mathfrak{n},\xi))
is the subspace of ordinary
\Lambda
-adic forms of tame level
\mathfrak{n}
and Nebentypus
\xi
. For any finite flat
\Lambda
-algebra
R
, we denote by

{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi,R):={\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)\otimes_{\Lambda}R

and let
{\bf T}(\mathfrak{n},\xi,R):=R[T_{\mathfrak{l}},U_{\mathfrak{q}}]_{\mathfrak{l}\nmid\mathfrak{n}p,\mathfrak{q}\mid\mathfrak{n}p}\subset\mathrm{End}_{R}\left({\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi,R)\right)
be Hida’s big ordinary cuspidal Hecke algebra acting faithfully on
{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi,R)
. Hida’s control theorem
[
Hid88
]
(see also
[
Wil88
]
) yields:

•

{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)
is a finite free
\Lambda
-module.

•

The specialization
\nu_{k}:{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi)\to S^{{\mathrm{ord}}}_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k}):=e_{\mathrm{ord}}(S_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n}p,\xi\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k}))
is surjective.

•

{\mathbf{T}}(\mathfrak{n},\xi,\Lambda)
is finite and flat over
\Lambda
.

5.2.
Hida families of theta series

Let
K
be a totally imaginary quadratic extension of
F
as before and let
\tau_{K/F}:C_{F}\to\left\{\pm 1\right\}
be the quadratic character associated with
K/F
. To an idele class character
\psi:C_{K}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
of infinity type
k\Sigma
and conductor
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\psi}
prime to
p
, we can associate a theta series
\theta_{\psi}\in S_{k+1}(\mathrm{N}_{K/F}(\mathfrak{C}_{\psi})\mathfrak{d}_{K/F},\xi)
with
\xi:=\mid\cdot\mid_{\mathbf{A}_{F}}^{-k}\psi_{+}\tau_{K/F}
. The Fourier coefficients of
\theta_{\psi}
are given by

C({\mathfrak{a}},\theta_{\psi})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{A}:\text{ ideals of }\mathfrak{o}_{K},\\
\mathfrak{A}\overline{\mathfrak{A}}={\mathfrak{a}},\,(\mathfrak{A},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\psi})=1\end{subarray}}\psi(\mathfrak{A}).

The
p
-stabilized newform
\theta_{\psi}^{(\Sigma_{p})}
associated with
\theta_{\psi}
is defined by

C({\mathfrak{a}},\theta_{\psi}^{(\Sigma_{p})})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{A}:\text{ ideals of }\mathfrak{o}_{K},\\
\mathfrak{A}\overline{\mathfrak{A}}={\mathfrak{a}},\,(\mathfrak{A},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\psi}\Sigma_{p})=1\end{subarray}}\psi(\mathfrak{A}).

In particular, if
{\mathfrak{p}}
is a prime above
p
, then

C({\mathfrak{p}},\theta_{\psi}^{(\Sigma_{p})})=\psi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\quad\text{ if }\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\text{ and }{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}.

Let
\Lambda_{K}={\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)}\rrbracket
be the Iwasawa algebra associated with the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K
. Then
\Lambda_{K}
is regarded as a
\Lambda
-algebra via the transfer map
\mathscr{V}:\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)\to\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)
. Write
\sigma\mapsto[\sigma]
for the inclusion of group-like elements
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)\to\Lambda_{K}^{\times}
. Define the universal character
\boldsymbol{\Psi}:G_{K}\to\Lambda_{K}^{\times}
by

(5.1)

\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)=[\sigma|_{K_{\Sigma_{p}}}]^{-1}.

By definition,
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
is unramifiedd outside
\Sigma_{p}
. For any prime ideal
\mathfrak{Q}\not\in\Sigma_{p}
, we put

\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathfrak{Q})=\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{Q}}).

Let
\phi:C_{K}\to{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
be a ray class character of conductor
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi}
coprime to
p
. We define the ordinary
\Lambda_{K}
-adic cusp from
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\in{\bf S}^{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{K/F},\Lambda_{K})
with
\mathfrak{n}={\mathrm{N}}_{K/F}({\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi})\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}
by

C({\mathfrak{a}},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{A}:\text{ ideals of }\mathfrak{o}_{K},\\
\mathfrak{A}\overline{\mathfrak{A}}={\mathfrak{a}},\,(\mathfrak{A},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi}\Sigma_{p})=1\end{subarray}}\phi(\mathfrak{A})\cdot\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}})\in\Lambda_{K}.

Let
\psi
be the Hecke character of
K
such that
\widehat{\psi}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}
. By definition,

(5.2)

\nu_{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})=\iota_{p}(\theta_{\phi\psi^{-k}}^{(\Sigma_{p})})\in S^{\mathrm{ord}}_{k+1}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{K/F}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k}).

It follows that
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}
is a
\Lambda_{K}
-adic newform of tame conductor
\mathfrak{n}
in the sense of
[
Wil88
, Definition 1.5.1]
. For each prime
\mathfrak{l}
of
\mathfrak{o}_{F}
, we have

T_{\mathfrak{l}}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}=C(\mathfrak{l},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\text{ if }(\mathfrak{l},p\mathfrak{n})=1,\quad U_{\mathfrak{l}}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}=C(\mathfrak{l},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\text{ if }\mathfrak{l}\mid p\mathfrak{n},

where

(5.3)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{l},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}({\mathfrak{L}})+\tau_{K/F}(\mathfrak{l})\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})&\text{ if }(\mathfrak{l},p\mathfrak{n})=1\text{ and }{\mathfrak{L}}\mid\mathfrak{l}\mathfrak{o}_{K},\\
\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})&\text{ if }\mathfrak{l}\mathfrak{o}_{K}={\mathfrak{L}}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}\text{ with }\mathfrak{l}\mid p\mathfrak{n}\text{ and }{\mathfrak{L}}\mid\Sigma_{p}{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi},\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

5.3.
A
\Lambda
-adic form of Eisenstein series

Let
\zeta_{F,p}(s)
be the Deligne–Ribet
p
-adic
L
-function satisfying the interpolation property that for all positive integer
k
,

(5.4)

\zeta_{F,p}(1-k)=\left(\prod_{{\mathfrak{p}}|p}(1-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}({\mathfrak{p}})^{k}{\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{k-1})\right)L(1-k,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{F}^{k}).

Let
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\vee}_{F,p}\in\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits\Lambda
be the element in Iwasawa algebra such that

\left<{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}}\right>^{k}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\vee}_{F,p})=\zeta_{F,p}(1-k).

Let
\mathfrak{q}_{0}\nmid p
be a prime of
\mathfrak{o}
such that
\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}
generates the Galois
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(F_{\infty}/F)
. Put

H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}=1-[{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}]\in\Lambda.

For each integral ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
F
with
(\mathfrak{m},p)=1
,
we let
{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},1)
be the
\Lambda
-adic Eisenstein series with the
{\mathfrak{a}}
-th Fourier coefficient given by

(5.5)

C({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},1))=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}{\mathfrak{a}}\subset{\mathfrak{b}}\subset\mathfrak{o},\,(p,{\mathfrak{b}})=1}{\mathrm{N}}{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1}[{\rm Fr}_{{\mathfrak{b}}}]^{-1}\in\Lambda,

and the constant terms are given by

C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},1))=2^{-[F:\mathbf{Q}]}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\vee}_{F,p}

for
i=1,\ldots,h_{+}
. This way we see that
H_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}\cdot{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},1)\in\mathbf{M}(\mathfrak{m},\boldsymbol{\omega})
. Recall that if
\mathfrak{l}
is a prime factor of
\mathfrak{m}
, then
U_{\mathfrak{l}}\in\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits\mathbf{M}(\mathfrak{m},\xi)
is defined by

\displaystyle C({\mathfrak{a}},U_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathcal{F})=C(\mathfrak{l}{\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{F}),\quad C_{i}(0,U_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathcal{F})=C_{i}(0,\mathcal{F}).

For any square-free divisor
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\mid\mathfrak{m}
, we define

(5.6)

{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}_{0})=\prod_{\mathfrak{l}\mid\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\left(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}^{-1}U_{\mathfrak{l}}\right){\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},1).

In particular,

(5.7)

C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}_{0}))=2^{-[F:\mathbf{Q}]}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\vee}_{F,p}\prod_{\mathfrak{l}\mid\mathfrak{m}_{0}}(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}^{-1})\text{ for }i=1,\dots,h_{+}.

5.4.
The Rankin-Selberg convolution

We call a ray class character
\chi

anticyclotomic
if
\chi(c\sigma c)=\chi(\sigma^{-1})
, or equivalently
\chi\circ\mathscr{V}={\mathbf{1}}
. When regarded as a Hecke character,
\chi
is anticyclotomic if
\chi|_{\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}}={\mathbf{1}}
. Let
\mathfrak{f}
be an integral ideal of
\mathfrak{o}
. Let
\chi
be a primitive anticyclotomic character of
K
modulo
\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{o}_{K}
with value in
{\mathcal{W}}^{\times}
. Suppose that

(\mathfrak{f},p\mathfrak{d}_{K/F})=1.

There exists a ray class character
\phi
unramified at
p\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}
such that

\chi=\phi^{1-c}

(
cf.

[
Hid06
, Lemma 5.31]
). We are allowed to take a twist by a character of the form
\xi_{1}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{K/F}
with a ray class character
\xi_{1}
of
F
. We say a character
\eta:K_{v}^{\times}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is
minimal
if the conductor of
\eta
is minimal among
\eta\cdot\xi_{1}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{K_{v}/F_{v}}
, where
\xi_{1}
runs over ray class characters of
F
. Now we fix an auxiliary prime
\mathfrak{l}_{0}\nmid p\mathfrak{f}\Delta_{F}
of
\mathfrak{o}
which is split in
K
. With the help of Lemma
5.1
below, we may assume
\phi
satisfies the following minimal condition

(min)

\phi_{v}
is minimal for all
v\in{\bf h}
except for
v=\mathfrak{l}_{0}

by replacing
\phi
by
\phi\cdot\xi_{1}\circ{\mathrm{N}}_{K/F}
for a suitable ray class character
\xi_{1}
of
F
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
S
be a finite set of primes of
\mathfrak{o}
. Let
\xi_{S}
be a finite order character of
\mathfrak{o}_{S}^{\times}:=\prod_{v\in S}\mathfrak{o}_{v}^{\times}
. For any prime
\mathfrak{l}\not\in S
, there exists a finite order character
\xi:C_{F}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
such that
\xi|_{\mathfrak{o}_{S}^{\times}}=\xi_{S}
and
\xi
is unramified outside
S\cup\left\{\mathfrak{l}\right\}
.

Proof.
.

Let
U_{F}:=(F\otimes\mathbf{R})^{\times}\prod_{v\in{\bf h}}\mathfrak{o}_{v}^{\times}
and let
U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}=\left\{x\in U_{F}\mid x_{\mathfrak{l}}=1\right\}
be a closed subgroup of
U_{F}
. We first extend
\xi_{S}
to a character
\xi
of
U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}
so that
\xi
is trivial on the
v
-component of
U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}
for each
v\not\in S
. Since
\mathfrak{o}^{\times}\cap U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}=\left\{1\right\}
, we can further extend
\xi
to the product
\mathfrak{o}^{\times}U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}
by requiring
\xi|_{\mathfrak{o}^{\times}}=1
. Since the product
\mathfrak{o}^{\times}U_{F}^{(\mathfrak{l})}
is a closed subgroup in
U_{F}
, by Pontryagin duality
\xi
can be further extended to
U_{F}
which is trivial on
\mathfrak{o}^{\times}
. The image of
U_{F}
has finite index in
C_{F}=\mathbf{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}
, so we can extend
\xi
to
C_{F}
and obtain desired idele class character of finite order.
∎

The conductor
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi}
of
\phi
can be written as
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi}={\mathfrak{C}}_{0}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{m}
with
({\mathfrak{C}}_{0},\mathfrak{l}_{0})=1
. The minimal condition for
\phi
implies that
{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}
has a decomposition

{\mathfrak{C}}_{0}={\mathfrak{C}}^{+}{\mathfrak{C}}^{-},\quad({\mathfrak{C}}_{0},p\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}\mathfrak{l}_{0})=1;\,({\mathfrak{C}}^{+},\overline{{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}})=1,

where
{\mathfrak{C}}^{-}
is only divisible by primes inert in
K
and
{\mathfrak{C}}^{+}
is only divisible by primes split in
K
. Put

(5.8)

\mathfrak{n}:=\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}({\mathfrak{c}}^{-})^{2}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{m_{0}};\quad\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{d}_{K/F}{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{m_{0}}\mid\mathfrak{n},

where
{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}={\mathfrak{C}}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{o}
and
{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}={\mathfrak{C}}^{-}\cap\mathfrak{o}
. With the transfer map
\mathscr{V}:\Lambda\to\Lambda_{K}
, we define

(5.9)

{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0}):=\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{2^{d}\zeta_{F_{\mathfrak{l}_{0}}}(1)}{\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\vee}_{F,p}}\cdot{\mathcal{E}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0})\right).

By definition, we have

C_{i}(0,{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0}))=1\text{ for all }i=1,\dots,h_{+};\quad C({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0}))\in\Lambda_{P},

where
\Lambda_{P}
is the localization of
\Lambda_{K}
at the augmentation ideal
P
.
Let
\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}\in S_{1}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{K/F})
be the theta series of weight one defined by

(5.10)

C({\mathfrak{a}},\theta_{\phi}^{\circ})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathfrak{A},{\mathfrak{C}}_{\phi}\overline{\Sigma}_{p}^{\rm reg})=1,\\
{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{A}={\mathfrak{a}}\end{subarray}}\phi(\mathfrak{A}).

In particular, if
{\mathfrak{p}}
is a prime of
\mathfrak{o}_{F}
above
p
with
{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
and
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}
, then

C({\mathfrak{p}},\theta_{\phi}^{\circ})=\begin{cases}\phi(\mathfrak{P})&\text{ if }\mathfrak{P}\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr},\\
\phi(\mathfrak{P})+\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})&\text{ if }\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}.\end{cases}

We shall consider the spectral decomposition of the product

e_{\mathrm{ord}}(\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}\cdot{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0}))\in\mathbf{S}^{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{F/F},\Lambda_{P}).

To be more precise, we denote for simplicity
\mathbf{S}
for
\mathbf{S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{F/F},\Lambda_{K})
and let
\mathbf{K}
be the field of fractions of
\Lambda_{K}
. Let
\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
be a finite extension of
\mathbf{K}
containing all Fourier coefficients of normalized newforms in
{\bf S}^{{\mathrm{ord}}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\overline{\mathbf{K}}
. Let
\mathbf{S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}
be the subspace of
{\bf S}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\mathbf{K}^{\prime}
generated by

\left\{V_{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathcal{F}}\mid{\mathcal{F}}\text{ a new form of level }\mathfrak{m}\text{ in }{\bf S}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\mathbf{K}^{\prime}\text{ and }\mathfrak{m}{\mathfrak{b}}\mid\mathfrak{n},\,{\mathcal{F}}\neq\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi},\,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\right\}

and let
{\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}={\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}^{\prime}}\cap{\bf S}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\mathbf{K}
. Then it follows from the theory of newforms
[
Wil88
, Prop. 1.5.2]
that we have a spectral decomposition (a direct sum as Hecke modules)

(5.11)

\mathbf{S}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\mathbf{K}:=\mathbf{K}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\oplus\mathbf{K}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\oplus\mathbf{S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}.

Note that
\mathfrak{n}
is the tame conductor of
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}
, so
{\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}
is the space interpolating the orthogonal complement of the subspace spanned by classical specializations of
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}
under the Petersson inner product.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}(\phi,\phi^{c})
and
\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi)
be the unique elements in
\mathbf{K}
such that

(5.12)

\mathscr{H}:=e_{\mathrm{ord}}(\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0}))-\mathcal{C}(\phi,\phi^{c})\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}-\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi)\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\in{\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}.

Proposition 5.3
.

With the ray class character
\phi
and the ideal
\mathfrak{m}
as above, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi))=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left<{{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{m}}\right>^{s}}\cdot\frac{2^{d}{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,{\mathbf{1}}){\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,0,\chi)}{h_{K/F}\cdot{\mathcal{L}}_{\Sigma}(s,-s,\chi)\zeta_{F,p}(1-s)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{-s}(\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{d}_{K}^{2})}{\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}}(1-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}))\prod_{\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}}(1-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}))}\cdot{\rm Ex}_{\mathfrak{l}_{0}}(s),

where

{\rm Ex}_{\mathfrak{l}_{0}}(s)=\frac{(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\mathfrak{L}}_{0}^{-1}))(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}_{0}^{-1}))(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\chi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\mathfrak{L}}_{0}^{-1}))(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\chi\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}_{0}^{-1}))}{(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1})^{2}(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\chi({\mathfrak{L}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}({\mathfrak{L}}_{0}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}_{0}^{-1}))(1-{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak{l}_{0}^{-1}\chi(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{s}(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}_{0}{\mathfrak{L}}_{0}^{-1}))}.

In particular,
\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi)
and
\mathcal{C}(\phi,\phi^{c})
are both non-zero.

The proof of Proposition
5.3
will be given in §
7
.

6.
Modular construction of cohomology classes and the applications to
p
-adic
L
-functions

6.1.
Ribet’s construction of cohomology classes

Let
{\bf S}^{\perp}:={\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}\cap({\bf S}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}{\Lambda_{P}})
. Let
{\bf T^{\perp}}\subset\mathrm{End}_{\Lambda_{K}}(\mathbf{S}^{\perp})
be the image of the Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}={\mathbf{T}}(\mathfrak{n},\phi_{+}\tau_{K/\mathbf{Q}},\Lambda_{K})
restricted to
{\bf S}^{\perp}
. The computation of
[
DKV18
, §5]
provides a general method to compute the
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}
of
\chi
along an additive homomorphism
\psi:G_{K,S}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
introduced in Definition
2.2
if one can construct an explicit Hecke eigensystem of
{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
valued in some artinian local ring
W
.

Fix a topological generator
\gamma_{0}\in\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{\Sigma_{p}}/K)\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}
. We have
\Lambda_{K}={\mathcal{W}}\llbracket{X}\rrbracket
with the variable

(6.1)

X:=([\gamma_{0}]-1)/\log_{p}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\gamma_{0}))\in\Lambda_{K}.

Let
S
be a set of prime divisors of
p\mathfrak{n}
. For any finite extension
L/F
, denote by
G_{L,S}
the Galois group of the algebraic extension of
L
unramified outside primes above
S
. Let
r:=r_{\Sigma}(\chi)
. Suppose that
r>0
. Let
\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}=\left\{\mathfrak{P}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{P}_{r}\right\}
and put

\widetilde{\Lambda}:=\Lambda_{P}\left[Y,\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\dots,\varepsilon_{r}\right].

For
n\geq m\geq 1
,
z\in\Lambda_{P}
, and
b\in\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, let
I_{n,m,z,b}
be the ideal of
\widetilde{\Lambda}
defined by

I_{n,m,z,b}:=(Y^{n+1},Y(X-Y),X^{m}-zY^{m},X\varepsilon_{i},Y\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{i}^{2},\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}+bY^{n}).

We consider the artinian local ring
W=\widetilde{\Lambda}/I_{n,m,z,b}
with the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{W}=(X,Y,\varepsilon_{i})
. The following proposition is extracted from
[
DKV18
, §4 and §5]
with some modifications to our setting. Recall that
\boldsymbol{\Psi}:G_{K,\Sigma_{p}}\to\Lambda_{K}^{\times}
is the universal character defined in (
5.1
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\lambda:{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}\to W
be a surjective
\Lambda_{K}
-algebra homomorphism with
n\geq 0
such that for primes
\mathfrak{l}\not\in S
,

\displaystyle\lambda(T_{\mathfrak{l}})

\displaystyle\equiv\phi({\mathfrak{L}})+\phi(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{W}})\text{ if }\mathfrak{l}={\mathfrak{L}}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}\text{ split in }K,

\displaystyle\lambda(T_{\mathfrak{l}})

\displaystyle\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{W}})\text{ if $\mathfrak{l}$ is inert in $K$},

and for
{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
with
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}
,

\displaystyle\lambda(U_{\mathfrak{p}})

\displaystyle=\phi^{c}\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}})\text{ if }\mathfrak{P}\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr},

\displaystyle\lambda(U_{{\mathfrak{p}}})

\displaystyle=\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i})(1+\varepsilon_{i})\text{ if }\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}_{i}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}.

Suppose that there exists a character
\widetilde{\Psi}:G_{K,S}\to W^{\times}
and an element
Z\in(X,Y)
such that

(i)

\widetilde{\Psi}\equiv\boldsymbol{\Psi}+Z\psi\,(\mbox{mod }{Z\mathfrak{m}_{W}})
for some additive character
\psi:G_{K,S}\to{\mathcal{W}}
,

(ii)

\lambda(T_{\mathfrak{l}})=\phi\widetilde{\Psi}({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{L}})+\phi\widetilde{\Psi}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}})
for
\mathfrak{l}={\mathfrak{L}}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}
split in
K
with
\mathfrak{l}\not\in S
.

If (

Σ

-Leo
) holds, then we have the equation

-bY^{n}+\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}Z^{r}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{Z^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}}).

Proof.
.

Let
\Lambda^{\dagger}\supset\Lambda_{P}
be the local ring of rigid analytic functions around
X=0
, i.e.

\Lambda^{\dagger}=\left\{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}X^{n}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\llbracket{X}\rrbracket\,\middle|\text{ there exist $t>0$ such that }\lim_{n\to\infty}\left|a_{n}\right|t^{n}=0\right\}.

Let
{\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}={\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}\otimes_{\Lambda_{K}}\Lambda^{\dagger}
be a finite
\Lambda^{\dagger}
-algebra. Let
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=\widetilde{\Lambda}\otimes_{\Lambda_{P}}\Lambda^{\dagger}
and
I=I_{n,m,z,b}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\dagger}
. Let
{\bf I}=\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\lambda
be the kernel of the map
\lambda:{\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}\to\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\dagger}/I
. Note that
{\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}/{\bf I}\simeq\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\dagger}/I
is a local ring. Let
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
be the maximal ideal of
{\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}
containing
{\bf I}
and denote by
R={\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}_{(\mathfrak{m}_{R})}
the localization of
{\mathbf{T}}^{\dagger}
at
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
. Then
R
is a finite flat
\Lambda^{\dagger}
-algebra. In addition,
R
is reduced for
\mathfrak{n}
is the tame conductor of
\theta_{\phi}
. The theory of pseudo-characters produces a continuous irreducible Galois representation
\rho_{\lambda}:G_{F,S}\to\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(R)
such that
\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\rho_{\lambda}({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{l}})=T_{\mathfrak{l}}\in R
for any prime
\mathfrak{l}\not\in S
and the assumption (ii) implies that

(6.2)

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\rho_{\lambda}|_{G_{K,S}}\equiv\phi\widetilde{\Psi}+\phi^{c}\widetilde{\Psi}^{c}\,(\mbox{mod }{{\bf I}R}).

The fractional field
\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R
is isomorphic to a product of fields

\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R=\prod_{i=1}^{t}L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}},

and each field
L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}
is a finite extension of
\Lambda^{\dagger}
and corresponds to a cuspidal Hida family
\mathscr{H}_{i}
. For
i=1,\dots,t
, let
\pi_{i}:\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R\to L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}
be the natural projection map. Then
\rho_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}:=\rho_{\lambda}\circ\pi_{i}:G_{F,S}\to\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}})
is the Galois representation associated with
\mathscr{H}_{i}
. We claim the restriction
\rho_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}|_{G_{K,S}}
is still irreducible. Otherwise
\mathscr{H}_{i}
would be the Hida family
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi_{1}}
of CM forms in
\mathrm{Spec}\,{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
for some ray class character
\phi_{1}\neq\phi
or
\phi^{c}
whose specialization at some arithmetic point
P^{\prime}
above
P
agree with
\theta_{\phi}^{(\Sigma_{p})}
, which in turns suggests that
\phi_{1}+\phi_{1}^{c}=\phi+\phi^{c}
, and
\phi_{1}=\phi
or
\phi^{c}
, a contradiction.

By
[
HT94
, Theorem 6.12]
, for each
{\mathfrak{p}}\mid p
, the subspace
L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}^{2}
fixed by the inertia group
\rho_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}(I_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
is one-dimensional over
L_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}
. Let
v_{\mathscr{H}_{i},{\mathfrak{p}}}
be a basis. Since
\mathscr{H}_{i}
are not Hida families of CM forms, the Galois representations
\rho_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}|_{G_{K,S}}
are irreducible. Combining the
\Sigma
-Leopoldt’s conjecture (

Σ

-Leo
) and
\phi\neq\phi^{c}
, we can apply the argument
[
DKV18
, Lemma 4.3]
shows that there exists
\sigma_{0}\in G_{K,S}
such that
\phi(\sigma_{0})\neq\phi(c\sigma_{0}c)
and
v_{\mathscr{H}_{i},{\mathfrak{p}}}
is not an eigenvector for all
\mathscr{H}_{i}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Choosing a basis
\left\{v_{1},v_{2}\right\}
consisting of eigenvectors of
\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma_{0})
for the representation
\rho_{\lambda}
, we may assume

(6.3)

\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma_{0})=\begin{pmatrix}{\nu_{1}}&0\\
0&{\nu_{2}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\nu_{1}\equiv\phi(\sigma_{0})\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{R}});\quad\nu_{2}\equiv\phi(c\sigma_{0}c)\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{R}}).

The image
\rho_{\lambda}(R[G_{K,S}])
of the group ring
R[G_{K,S}]
is of the form

\rho_{\lambda}(R[G_{K,S}])=\begin{pmatrix}{R}&{R_{12}}\\
{R_{21}}&{R}\end{pmatrix}\subset\mathrm{M}_{2}(R),

where
R_{ij}
are ideals in
R
; this is a generalized matrix algebra in the sense of
[
BC09
, Theorem 1.4.4]
. Note that
R_{12}
is a faithful
R
-module since
\rho_{\mathscr{H}_{i}}|_{G_{K,S}}
is irreducible for all
i
. Writing

\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma)=\begin{pmatrix}{a(\sigma)}&{b(\sigma)}\\
{c(\sigma)}&{d(\sigma)}\end{pmatrix}\text{ for }\sigma\in G_{K,S},

it follows from (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) that

a(\sigma)\equiv\phi\widetilde{\Psi}(\sigma)\,(\mbox{mod }{{\bf I}}),\quad d(\sigma)\equiv\phi\widetilde{\Psi}(c\sigma c)\,(\mbox{mod }{{\bf I}});\quad R_{12}R_{21}\subset{\bf I}R.

For each
{\mathfrak{p}}\mid p
, the
\rho_{\lambda}(I_{\mathfrak{p}})
-fixed vector
v_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=(v_{\mathscr{H}_{i},{\mathfrak{p}}})_{i=1,\dots,t}
is an eigenvector of
\rho_{\lambda}(G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}})
such that
\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma)v_{\mathfrak{p}}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\sigma)v_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where

\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathfrak{p}}:G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
is the unramified character with
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathfrak{p}}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}})=U_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Writing
v_{\mathfrak{p}}=A_{\mathfrak{p}}v_{1}+C_{\mathfrak{p}}v_{2}

with
(C_{\mathfrak{p}},D_{\mathfrak{p}})\in(\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R)^{2}
, we obtain an invertible matrix
\begin{pmatrix}{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\\
{C_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{D_{\mathfrak{q}}}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R)
such that

\begin{pmatrix}{a(\sigma)}&{b(\sigma)}\\
{c(\sigma)}&{d(\sigma)}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\\
{C_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{D_{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\\
{C_{\mathfrak{p}}}&{D_{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\sigma)}&{*}\\
{0}&{*}\end{pmatrix}\text{ for all }\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}.

It follows that

(6.4)

C_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot b(\sigma)=A_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\sigma)-a(\sigma))\text{ for }\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}.

Note that
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
C_{\mathfrak{p}}
both belong to
(\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits R)^{\times}
since
v_{\mathscr{H}_{i},{\mathfrak{p}}}
is not an eigenvector of
\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma_{0})
for each
i
. Define the function

\mathscr{K}:G_{K,S}\to R_{12},\quad\mathscr{K}(\sigma)=b(\sigma)/d(\sigma).

For any
R
-submodule
R^{\prime}\supset\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}
of
R_{12}
, the reduction of
\mathscr{K}
modulo
R^{\prime}

\overline{\mathscr{K}}:=b/d\,(\mbox{mod }{R^{\prime}})=\phi^{-c}b\,(\mbox{mod }{R^{\prime}}):G_{K,S}\to R_{12}/R^{\prime}

is a continuous one-cocycle in
Z^{1}(G_{K,S},\chi\otimes R_{12}/R^{\prime})
. We claim that if the class
[\overline{\mathscr{K}}]\in{\rm H}^{1}(K,\chi\otimes R_{12}/R^{\prime})
represented by
\overline{\mathscr{K}}
is zero, then
R_{12}=R^{\prime}
. We can write
b(\sigma)\,(\mbox{mod }{R^{\prime}})=(\phi^{c}(\sigma)-\phi(\sigma))z
for some
z\in R_{12}/R^{\prime}
. Evaluating
b(\sigma)
at
\sigma=\sigma_{0}
, we immediately see that
z=0
from (
6.3
) and hence
b(\sigma)\,(\mbox{mod }{R^{\prime}})
is zero. Since
R_{12}
is the
R
-module generated by
\left\{b(\sigma)\right\}_{\sigma\in G_{K,S}}
, we conclude
R_{12}=R^{\prime}
. In particular, this shows that
\mathscr{K}\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}})
represents a non-zero class

(6.5)

\kappa_{\lambda}:=[\overline{\mathscr{K}}]\in{\rm H}^{1}(K,\chi\otimes R_{12}/\mathfrak{m}_{R}).

Let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be a prime of
\mathfrak{o}
above
p
and write
{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{o}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
with
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{P}\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}=\left\{\mathfrak{P}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{P}_{r}\right\}
, i.e.
\phi\neq\phi^{c}
on
G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}
. Then there exists
\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}})-a(\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}})\in R^{\times}
. We thus obtain

\mathscr{K}(\sigma)=\frac{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}{C_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\frac{(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\sigma)-a(\sigma))}{d(\sigma)},

and
\overline{\mathscr{K}}(\sigma)=\frac{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}{C_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1-\chi(\sigma))\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}})
for all
\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}}
. This shows that the class
[\overline{\mathscr{K}}]
is locally trivial at
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
for any
\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
. Let
R^{\prime}_{12}
be the submodule of
R_{12}
generated by
\left\{\mathscr{K}(\sigma)\right\}_{\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}}}
with
\mathfrak{P}_{i}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
. Then
\overline{\mathscr{K}}:G_{K}\to R_{12}/(\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}+R^{\prime}_{12})
is a cocycle whose class
[\overline{\mathscr{K}}]
is locally trivial outside
\Sigma_{p}
, and we see that
[\overline{\mathscr{K}}]=0
by the
\Sigma
-Leopodlt conjecture (

Σ

-Leo
). From the above claim, we obtain that
\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}+R^{\prime}_{12}=R_{12}
and hence
R^{\prime}_{12}=R_{12}
by Nakayama’s lemma. Let
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}=\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}^{-1})U_{p}-1\in R
and
\widetilde{Z}\in R
be liftings of
\varepsilon_{i}
and
Z
respectively. Then the assumption (i) implies that
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}(\sigma)-a(\sigma)\in\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}R+\widetilde{Z}R+{\bf I}
for
\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}}
. Therefore, we conclude that

(6.6)

R_{12}\subset\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{A_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}{C_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}(\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}R+\widetilde{Z}R+{\bf I}).

For each integral prime
\mathfrak{Q}
of
K
, denote by

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathfrak{Q}}:{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{Q}},\chi\otimes R_{12}/\mathfrak{m}_{R})\times{\rm H}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{Q}},\chi^{-1}(1))\to R_{12}/\mathfrak{m}_{R}

the local Tate pairing. We choose a basis
\left\{u_{1},u_{2},\dots,u_{r}\right\}
of
{\rm H}^{1}_{(0,\emptyset)}(K,\chi^{-1}(1))
.
Since the class
\kappa_{\lambda}
is unramified outside
\Sigma_{p}\cup\overline{\Sigma}_{p}^{\rm irr}
, we obtain

(6.7)

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\langle{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}(\kappa_{\lambda}),{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}(u_{j})\rangle_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}=0\text{ in }R_{12}/\mathfrak{m}_{R}.

For
\sigma\in G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi^{c}\widetilde{\Psi}^{c}(\sigma^{-1})(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\sigma)-a(\sigma))

\displaystyle\equiv\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)(1+\varepsilon_{i})^{o_{i}(\sigma)}-(\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)+\psi(\sigma)Z)

\displaystyle\equiv o_{i}(\sigma)\varepsilon_{i}-\psi(\sigma)Z\,(\mbox{mod }{{\bf I}+\widetilde{Z}\mathfrak{m}_{R}}).

Put
o_{ij}=\langle{\mathrm{ord}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}},{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}(u_{j})\rangle_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}
and
\psi_{ij}=\langle{\rm loc}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}(\psi),{\rm loc}_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}}(u_{j})\rangle_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}
. According to Definition
2.2
,

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}:=(-1)^{r}\frac{\det\left((\psi_{ij})\right)}{\det\left((o_{ij})\right)}.

It follows from (
6.7
) that

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{A_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}{C_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}(o_{ij}\widetilde{\epsilon}_{i}-\psi_{ij}\widetilde{Z}+m_{ij}^{\prime})\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{\mathfrak{m}_{R}R_{12}})\text{ for some }m^{\prime}_{ij}\in{\bf I}+\widetilde{Z}\mathfrak{m}_{R}.

Combining (
6.6
), we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{A_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}{C_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}}(o_{ij}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}-\psi_{ij}\widetilde{Z}+m_{ij})=0\text{ for some }m_{ij}\in{\bf I}+\widetilde{Z}\mathfrak{m}_{R}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\mathfrak{m}_{R}.

Hence,

\det(o_{ij}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}-\psi_{ij}\widetilde{Z}+m_{ij})=0.

Applying
\lambda
on both sides, we obtain

\det(o_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}-\psi_{ij}Z+\lambda(m_{ij}))=0,\quad\lambda(m_{ij})\in(Z,\varepsilon_{i})\mathfrak{m}_{W}.

Since
\varepsilon_{i}Z=\varepsilon_{i}\mathfrak{m}_{W}^{r}=0
, we find that that

\displaystyle\det(o_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}-\psi_{ij}Z+\lambda(m_{ij}))

\displaystyle\equiv\det(o_{ij})\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}+(-1)^{r}\det(\psi_{ij})Z^{r}\,(\mbox{mod }{Z^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}})

\displaystyle\equiv\det(o_{ij})\left(-bY^{n}+\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\psi}Z^{r}\right)\,(\mbox{mod }{Z^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}}).

The proposition follows immediately.
∎

6.2.
Applications to the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
\chi
be a non-trivial anticyclotoic character of conductor
\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{o}_{K}
as in §
5.4
. Recall that in the
\Lambda
-adic cusp form
\mathscr{H}
in Definition
5.2
is given by

\mathscr{H}=e_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0})\cdot\theta^{\circ}_{\phi})-\frac{1}{B}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-\frac{1}{A}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\in{\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}.

with
A=\mathcal{C}(\phi,\phi^{c})^{-1}
and
B=\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi)^{-1}
in
\mathbf{K}^{\times}=(\mathop{\rm Frac}\nolimits\Lambda_{P})^{\times}
. We shall assume that the Leopoldt conjecture for
F
holds, which is, by a result of Colmez
[
Col88
]
, equivalent to the following

(
F
-Leo)

\zeta_{F,p}(s)\text{ has a simple pole at $s=1$}\iff\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{F,p}^{\vee}}\in X\Lambda_{P}.

By the constant term formula (
5.7
) and the definition of the
\Lambda
-adic Eisenstein series
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0})
in (
5.9
), we find that (

F

-Leo
) implies that

(6.8)

{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0})\equiv 1\,(\mbox{mod }{X}).

We continue to assume that
r=r_{\Sigma}(\chi)>0
in this subsection. For
i=1,\dots,r
, we put

\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}:=\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}}^{-1})U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}-1\in{\mathbf{T}}.

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume that (

F

-Leo
) and (

Σ

-Leo
) hold. Then we have

B\in X\Lambda_{P}.

Proof.
.

Suppose that
B\not\in X\Lambda_{P}
. Namely,
B^{-1}\in\Lambda_{P}
. There are two cases:

Case (i):
A^{-1}\in\Lambda_{P}
.
Let
\widetilde{\Lambda}
act on
{\bf S}^{\perp}
by

(6.9)

\varepsilon_{i}\cdot\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\mathscr{F};\quad Y\cdot\mathscr{F}=\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}\cdot\mathscr{F}.

Define the ideal
I
of
\widetilde{\Lambda}
by

I:=I_{1,1,0,-1}=(X,\varepsilon_{i}^{2},\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}-Y)

and
W:=\widetilde{\Lambda}/I
.
Then one verifies immediately that
\mathscr{F}_{0}:=\mathscr{H}\,(\mbox{mod }{X})
is annihilated and
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{0}
is a free of
W
-module of rank one. We have a homomorphism
\lambda:{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}\to W
with

\displaystyle\lambda(T_{\mathfrak{l}})

\displaystyle=\phi({\mathfrak{L}})+\phi(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})\text{ if }\mathfrak{l}={\mathfrak{L}}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}\text{ is split in $K$},

\displaystyle\lambda(T_{\mathfrak{l}})

\displaystyle=0\text{ if $\mathfrak{l}$ is inert in $K$},

\displaystyle\lambda(U_{{\mathfrak{p}}})

\displaystyle=\phi(\mathfrak{P})\text{ if }{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}\text{ with }\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr},

\displaystyle\lambda(U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}})

\displaystyle=\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i})(1+\varepsilon_{i})\text{ if }{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}=\mathfrak{P}_{i}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}\text{ with }\mathfrak{P}_{i}\in\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}.

Applying Proposition
6.1
to
\lambda
with
\widetilde{\Psi}=1
,
Z=X
and
\psi=0
, we find that

Y\in X^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}=\left\{0\right\}.

This is a contradiction.

Case (ii):
A\in P\Lambda_{P}
. Let
r_{A}={\mathrm{ord}}_{P}(A)\geq 1
and
a^{*}=A/X^{r_{A}}|_{X=0}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
. We let
\widetilde{\Lambda}
act on
{\bf S}^{\perp}/(X^{r_{A}+1})
by

Y\cdot\mathscr{F}=X\mathscr{F},\quad\varepsilon_{i}\cdot\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\mathscr{F}.

Put
\mathscr{H}^{\prime}:=-A\mathscr{H}\in{\bf S}^{\perp}
. Then by (
6.8
),

\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{H}^{\prime}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{A}+1}})=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}+\frac{A}{B}\theta^{(\Sigma_{p})}_{\phi}-A\theta^{\circ}_{\phi}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{A}+1}}).

Define the ideal
I
by

I:=I_{r_{A},1,1,a^{*}}=(Y^{r_{A}+1},X-Y,Y\varepsilon_{i},X\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{i}^{2},\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}+a^{*}Y^{r_{A}})

and
W=\widetilde{\Lambda}/I
.
Then one verifies that
\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}
is annihilated by the ideal
I
. Note that
\left\{Y^{i}\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{A}-1}
and
\left\{\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}\right\}_{J\neq\emptyset}
is a basis of
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}
and that
W
is generated by the
\Lambda_{P}
-algebra
W
is generated by
\left\{Y^{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{A}-1}
and the products
\varepsilon_{J}
with
J\neq\emptyset
over
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We thus conclude that
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}
is a free
W
-module of rank one. Using (
5.3
), we verify easily hat
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}
is an invariant
{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
-submodule with

\displaystyle T_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{L}}})+\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}}))\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}\text{ if $\mathfrak{l}={\mathfrak{L}}\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}$ is split},\quad T_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}=0\text{ if $\mathfrak{l}$ is inert};

\displaystyle U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i})(1+\varepsilon_{i})\mathscr{F}_{0}^{\prime}.

Applying Proposition
6.1
again with
\widetilde{\Psi}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}
,
Z=Y^{r_{A}}
and
\psi=0
, we get

Y^{r_{A}}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{Y^{r_{A}}\mathfrak{m}_{W}}).

This is a contradiction.
∎

For
\sigma\in G_{K}
, the universal character
\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{2}})
in (
5.1
) can be written as

(6.10)

\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)=1+\eta_{\Sigma}(\sigma)X\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{2}})

for some homomorphism
\eta_{\Sigma}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(G_{K,S},{\mathcal{W}})
. By definition and the choice of
X
in (
6.1
), we have

\eta_{\Sigma}(\sigma)=-\log_{p}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}(\sigma|_{K_{\Sigma_{p}}})).

Recall that in Definition
2.2
we have introduced the anticyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant

(6.11)

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\ell^{\rm ac}}

along the
anticyclotomic logarithm

\ell^{\rm ac}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits(G_{K,S},\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
given by

\ell^{\rm ac}=\log_{p}\circ\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\Sigma}^{1-c}=\eta_{\overline{\Sigma}}-\eta_{\Sigma}.

We next apply Proposition
6.1
to relate the anticyclotomic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}
to the higher order derivatives of
B
.

Proposition 6.3
.

With the Leopoldt hypotheses (

F

-Leo
) and (

Σ

-Leo
), we have

\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=\frac{B}{X^{r}}\bigg|_{X=0}.

Proof.
.

Write
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{l}_{0})
and consider

\mathscr{H}=e_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathcal{G}}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ})-\frac{1}{B}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-\frac{1}{A}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}\in{\bf S}^{\perp}_{\mathbf{K}}.

By Proposition
6.2
,
B=\mathcal{C}(\phi^{c},\phi)^{-1}\in X\Lambda_{P}
. We set
r_{A}={\mathrm{ord}}_{P}(A)
and
r_{B}={\mathrm{ord}}_{P}(B)\geq 1
. Put

b^{*}:=\frac{B}{X^{r_{B}}}\Big|_{X=0}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}.

In what follows,
\mathfrak{l}
denotes an integral prime of
F
with
(\mathfrak{l},p\mathfrak{m})=1
and
{\mathfrak{L}}
is a prime of
K
above
\mathfrak{l}
. Let
\tau_{K/F}
be the quadratic ideal character of
F
associated with
K/F
. Choose a prime-to-
p
prime ideal
\mathfrak{q}_{0}=\mathfrak{Q}_{0}\overline{\mathfrak{Q}}_{0}
split in
K
such that

\phi(\mathfrak{Q}_{0})-\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{Q}}_{0})\neq 0\text{ and }\ell^{\rm ac}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{Q}}_{0}})\neq 0.

This implies that
C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})-C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}})\in X\Lambda^{\times}_{P}
. Define the Hecke operators
Y_{\phi}
and
Y_{\phi^{c}}
in
{\mathbf{T}}_{P}
by

(6.12)

\displaystyle Y_{\phi}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(T_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}-C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}))X}{C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}})-C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})},

\displaystyle Y_{\phi^{c}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(T_{\mathfrak{q}_{0}}-C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}))X}{C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi})-C(\mathfrak{q}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}})}.

Case (I):
\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}\neq\Sigma_{p}

Let
\widetilde{\Lambda}
act on
{\bf S}^{\perp}/(X^{r_{B}+1})
by

(6.13)

Y\cdot\mathscr{F}=Y_{\phi}\mathscr{F};\quad\varepsilon_{i}\cdot\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{i}\mathscr{F}.

Let
\mathfrak{P}_{0}\in\Sigma_{p}-\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}\mathfrak{o}_{K}=\mathfrak{P}_{0}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{0}
. Put

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\frac{-B}{\phi(\mathfrak{P}_{0})-\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{0})}(U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}}-\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{0}))\mathscr{H}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-B\cdot e_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathcal{G}}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ})\in{\bf S}^{\perp};

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{1}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}).

By (
6.8
), we have

\mathscr{F}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-b^{*}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}X^{r_{B}}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}).

One verifies that

\displaystyle Y\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}=X\mathscr{F}_{1}=X\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}=-b^{*}X^{r_{B}}\mathscr{F}_{1},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}=B\phi(\mathfrak{P}_{i}^{-2})(U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}-\phi(\mathfrak{P}_{i}))^{2}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}})=0.

Thus
\mathscr{F}_{1}
is annihilated by the ideal

I:=I_{r_{B},1,1,b^{*}}=(X^{r_{B}+1},Y-X,\varepsilon_{i}^{2},X\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{2}\dots\varepsilon_{r}+b^{*}X^{r_{B}})\subset\widetilde{\Lambda}.

Let
W=\widetilde{\Lambda}/I
. For each subset
J
of
\left\{1,2\dots,r\right\}
, put
\varepsilon_{J}:=\prod_{i\in J}\varepsilon_{i}
. It is easy to see that
W
is generated by the
\Lambda_{P}
-algebra
W
is generated by
\left\{X^{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{B}-1}
and the products
\varepsilon_{J}
with
J\neq\emptyset
over
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We claim that
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}
is a free
W
-module of rank one. To see it, it suffices to show that
\left\{X^{i}\mathscr{F}_{1}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{B}-1}
and
\left\{\varepsilon_{J}\mathscr{F}_{1}\right\}_{J\neq\emptyset}
are linearly independent. Suppose that there exists a polynomial
f(X)
with
\deg f<r_{B}
and
\alpha_{J}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
with
J\subset\left\{1,\dots,r\right\}
such that

f(X)\mathscr{F}_{1}+\sum_{J\subset\left\{1,\dots,r\right\},\,J\neq\emptyset}\alpha_{J}\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}=0.

We must show that
f(X)=0
and
\alpha_{J}=0
. Applying
Y
on both sides, we get

Xf(X)\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}).

Noting that
C(\mathfrak{o},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}})=1
, we obtain
Xf(X)\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}})
. This implies that
f(X)=0
and

0=\sum_{J\subset\left\{1,\dots,r\right\},\,J\neq\emptyset}\alpha_{J}\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}=X^{r_{B}}\left(\sum_{J\neq\emptyset}\alpha_{J}\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}\right)\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}).

Now the claim follows from the linear independence of the set
\left\{\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}\right\}_{J}
of modular forms.
From (
5.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle T_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathscr{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(\phi({\mathfrak{L}})\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}})+\tau_{K/F}(\mathfrak{l})\phi(\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}})\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{L}}})\right)\mathscr{F}_{1};

\displaystyle U_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathscr{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\phi(\mathfrak{P})\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}})\mathscr{F}_{1},\quad{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}\text{ with }\mathfrak{P}\in\Sigma_{p}\backslash\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr},

\displaystyle U_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}}\mathscr{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\phi\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\mathop{\rm Fr}\nolimits_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}})(1+\varepsilon_{i})\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}\text{ for }i=1,\dots,r.

This shows that the free
W
-module
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}
is a
{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
-invariant submodule of
{\bf S}^{\perp}/(X^{r_{B}+1})
. This induces a surjective
\Lambda_{P}
-alegbra homomorphism
\lambda:{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}\to W
such that

t\mathscr{F}_{1}=\lambda(t)\cdot\mathscr{F}_{1}
. This
\lambda
satisfies the assumptions in Proposition
6.1
with
\widetilde{\Psi}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{c}
,
Z=X
and
\psi=\ell^{\rm ac}
. Applying Proposition
6.1
, we obtain

-b^{*}X^{r_{B}}+\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}\cdot X^{r}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}}).

This implies that
r_{B}\geq r
and that
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=0
if
r_{B}>r
and
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=b^{*}
if
r_{B}=r
.

Case (II):
\Sigma_{p}=\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
and
r_{B}\geq r_{A}
.

Define the
\Lambda_{P}
-adic cuspform

\mathscr{H}_{2}:=(-B)\cdot\mathscr{H}=\frac{B}{A}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-B\cdot e_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathcal{G}}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}).

Let
\mathscr{F}_{2}:=\mathscr{H}_{2}\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}})
. Let
Y
and
\varepsilon_{i}
act on
\widetilde{\Lambda}
as in (
6.13
) and set
z_{2}=1+\frac{B}{A}\in\Lambda_{P}
. Then one verifies that

\displaystyle Y\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=X\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}},\quad Y\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}=X\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}},

\displaystyle X^{r_{A}}\mathscr{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=z_{2}Y^{r_{A}}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=-b^{*}X^{r_{B}}\theta_{\phi}^{(\Sigma_{p})}=-b^{*}Y^{r_{B}}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}.

This shows that
\mathscr{F}_{2}
is annihilated by the ideal

I:=I_{r_{B},r_{A},z_{2},b^{*}}=(Y^{r_{B}+1},\varepsilon_{i}^{2},X\varepsilon_{i},Y\varepsilon_{i},Y(X-Y),X^{r_{A}}-z_{2}Y^{r_{A}},\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{r}+b^{*}Y^{r_{B}}).

Let
W=\widetilde{\Lambda}/I
. We claim that
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}
is a free
W
-module. To see it, since
W/I
is generated by
\left\{X^{i}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{A}-1}

\left\{Y^{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{r_{B}-1}
and
\left\{\varepsilon_{J}\right\}_{J\neq\emptyset}
, it suffices to show that

\left\{X^{i}\mathscr{F}_{2}\right\}_{i=0}^{r_{A}-1}\cup\left\{Y^{j}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}\right\}_{j=1}^{r_{B}-1}\cup\left\{\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}\right\}_{J\subset\Sigma_{p},\,J\neq\emptyset,\Sigma_{p}}

are linearly independent over
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Suppose that we have a linear equation

f(X)\mathscr{H}_{2}+g(Y)Y\cdot\mathscr{H}_{2}+\sum_{J\neq\emptyset}\alpha_{J}\varepsilon_{J}\cdot\mathscr{H}_{2}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}})

for some polynomials
f(X),g(X)\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}[X]
with
\deg f<r_{A}
and
\deg g<r_{B}-1
. Applying
Y
on both sides, we obtain

(f(X)+g(X)X)X\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}+1}}),

and hence
f(X)+g(X)X\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}}})
as
C(\mathfrak{o},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}})=1
. It follows that

(6.14)

\displaystyle f(X)+g(X)X=0;

\displaystyle f(X)X^{r_{B}-r_{A}}u\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}+\sum_{J\neq\emptyset}\alpha_{J}b^{*}X^{r_{B}}\varepsilon_{J}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}=0,\quad u=\frac{B}{AX^{r_{B}-r_{A}}}\in\Lambda_{P}^{\times}.

We this find that
f(X)X^{r_{B}-r_{A}}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{X^{r_{B}}})
, which in turn shows that
f(X)=0
for
\deg f<r_{A}
. In view of (
6.14
), we thus get
g(X)=0
and
\alpha_{J}=0
for
J\neq\emptyset
. On the other hand, a direct calculation shows that

T_{\mathfrak{l}}\mathscr{F}_{2}=(\phi\Psi_{2}({\rm Fr}_{{\mathfrak{L}}})+\tau_{K/F}(\mathfrak{l})\phi\Psi_{2}({\rm Fr}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}}))\mathscr{F}_{2},

where
\Psi_{2}:G_{K,S}\to W^{\times}
is the character defined by

\Psi_{2}(\sigma)=\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)+\frac{\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{c}(\sigma)-\boldsymbol{\Psi}(\sigma)}{X}Y.

This shows that
W\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}
is an invariant
{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}
-submodule. We therefore obtain the surjective homomorphism
\lambda:{\mathbf{T}}^{\perp}\to W
such that
t\mathscr{F}_{2}=\lambda(t)\cdot\mathscr{F}_{2}
.
Applying Proposition
6.1
to
\lambda
with
\widetilde{\Psi}=\Psi_{2}
,
Z=Y
and
\psi=\ell^{\rm ac}
, we find that

-b^{*}Y^{r_{B}}+\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}Y^{r}\equiv 0\,(\mbox{mod }{Y^{r}\mathfrak{m}_{W}}).

This implies that
r_{B}\geq r
and that
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=0
if
r_{B}>r
and
\mathscr{L}_{\chi}^{\rm ac}=b^{*}
if
r_{B}=r
.

Case (III):
\Sigma_{p}=\Sigma_{p}^{\rm irr}
and
r_{A}>r_{B}>0

Let
a^{*}=\frac{A}{X^{r_{A}}}|_{X=0}\in\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Put

\mathscr{H}_{3}:=(-A)\mathscr{H}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi}+\frac{A}{B}\cdot\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\phi^{c}}-A\cdot e_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathcal{G}}\theta_{\phi}^{\circ}).

Let
W
act
{\bf S}^{\perp}/(X^{r_{A}+1})
by
